{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MFtBgx8xx8xiZzPMBnBSrg","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# EduPay Ledger Academy  \n     3→*A production-grade, clean-architecture fintech platform written in C.*\n     4→\n     5→---\n     6→\n     7→<img src=\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edupay/ledger-academy/main/docs/assets/edupay_logo.svg\" alt=\"EduPay Ledger Academy\" width=\"240\" />\n     8→\n     9→EduPay Ledger Academy is a **reference implementation** of enterprise-level payment rails tailored for higher-education.  \n    10→Unlike toy examples, the codebase powers real tuition, stipend, and scholarship flows **while doubling as a living textbook** for computer-science curricula.\n    11→\n    12→* Clean Architecture (Robert C. Martin) – every policy is isolated from frameworks.  \n    13→* Domain-Driven Design – bounded contexts for Admissions, Bursar, Financial-Aid, and Continuing-Education.  \n    14→* Event Sourcing + CQRS – immutable audit trail and low-latency read models.  \n    15→* Saga Orchestration – optional chaos-engineering labs for distributed rollbacks.  \n    16→* PCI-DSS / FERPA / PSD2 compliance patterns baked-in.\n    17→\n    18→> “We don’t hide complexity from students—we instrument it.”  \n    19→> — *Academic Advisory Board, 2024*\n    20→\n    21→---\n    22→\n    23→## Repository Layout\n    24→\n    25→```\n    26→EduPayLedgerAcademy/\n    27→├─ cmd/                     # CLI entrypoints and service launchers\n    28→│  ├─ ep_ledgerd/           # Monolithic daemon (default boot path)\n    29→│  └─ ep_saga_simulator/    # Demonstration CLI for saga experiments\n    30→├─ core/                    # Enterprise business rules (no I/O!)\n    31→│  ├─ admissions/\n    32→│  ├─ bursar/\n    33→│  ├─ financial_aid/\n    34→│  └─ continuing_education/\n    35→├─ drivers/                 # Secondary adapters (databases, brokers)\n    36→│  ├─ postgres/\n    37→│  ├─ rabbitmq/\n    38→│  └─ redis_cache/\n    39→├─ interfaces/              # Primary adapters (HTTP, GRPC, CLI, UI)\n    40→├─ docs/                    # Diagrams, course material, slide decks\n    41→└─ tests/                   # End-to-end, property, & fuzz tests\n    42→```\n    43→\n    44→The **core** folder contains *pure C* (C17) with zero external dependencies; all I/O is pushed outward to **drivers** to maintain testability and pedagogical clarity.\n    45→\n    46→---\n    47→\n    48→## Quick Start\n    49→\n    50→Prerequisites:\n    51→\n    52→* gcc / clang with C17 support  \n    53→* CMake ≥ 3.15  \n    54→* GNU Make (optional)  \n    55→* PostgreSQL 14, RabbitMQ 3.x (when running full stack)\n    56→\n    57→```bash\n    58→git clone https://github.com/edupay/ledger-academy.git\n    59→cd ledger-academy\n    60→cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release\n    61→cmake --build build --target all\n    62→./build/bin/ep_ledgerd --config etc/ledgerd.sample.toml\n    63→```\n    64→\n    65→Docker-compose is available for turnkey experimentation:\n    66→\n    67→```bash\n    68→docker compose -f infra/compose.dev.yml up --build\n    69→```\n    70→\n    71→---\n    72→\n    73→## Hello, World! Payment\n    74→\n    75→A minimal illustration of **command → domain → event** inside EduPay:\n    76→\n    77→```c\n    78→/* src/examples/tuition_payment.c */\n    79→\n    80→#include \"admissions/enroll_cmd.h\"\n    81→#include \"bursar/invoice_svc.h\"\n    82→#include \"eventbus/memory_bus.h\"\n    83→\n    84→int main(void)\n    85→{\n    86→    ep_event_bus_t *bus = ep_memory_bus_new();\n    87→    ep_invoice_service_t *svc = ep_invoice_service_new(bus);\n    88→\n    89→    const ep_enroll_cmd_t cmd = {\n    90→        .student_id = \"S12345\",\n    91→        .program_id = \"CS-BS\",\n    92→        .term = \"2024FALL\",\n    93→        .tuition_fee_cents = 1250000,   /* $12,500.00 */\n    94→        .currency = EP_CUR_USD\n    95→    };\n    96→\n    97→    if (ep_invoice_service_handle_enroll(svc, &cmd) != EP_OK) {\n    98→        fprintf(stderr, \"💥  Enrollment rejected: %s\\n\", ep_last_error());\n    99→        return EXIT_FAILURE;\n   100→    }\n   101→\n   102→    puts(\"✅  Invoice emitted; event stored in audit trail.\");\n   103→    ep_invoice_service_free(svc);\n   104→    ep_memory_bus_free(bus);\n   105→}\n   106→```\n   107→\n   108→```bash\n   109→gcc -std=c17 tuition_payment.c -I../include -L../lib -lep_core -o tuition_payment\n   110→./tuition_payment\n   111→```\n   112→\n   113→Output:\n   114→\n   115→```\n   116→✅  Invoice emitted; event stored in audit trail.\n   117→```\n   118→\n   119→---\n   120→\n   121→## Pedagogical Hooks\n   122→\n   123→| Module | In-Code Mentoring |\n   124→| ------ | ----------------- |\n   125→| `core/fraud_detection` | Inline heuristics annotated with links to academic papers. |\n   126→| `core/regulatory` | FERPA-aware pseudonymisation examples with unit tests. |\n   127→| `tests/fuzz` | AFL++ harnesses for students learning security testing. |\n   128→| `cmd/ep_saga_simulator` | Interactive CLI that severs message brokers mid-flight to demonstrate compensating transactions. |\n   129→\n   130→Activate the saga demo:\n   131→\n   132→```bash\n   133→./build/bin/ep_saga_simulator --scenario bursar_refund_outage\n   134→```\n   135→\n   136→---\n   137→\n   138→## Clean Architecture Overview\n   139→\n   140→```\n   141→          ┌────────────────────────────────┐\n   142→          │        Interface Layer        │\n   143→┌─HTTP────┤  (Controllers, gRPC, CLI)     │\n   144→│         └──────────────▲────────────────┘\n   145→│                        │\n   146→│         ┌──────────────┴────────────────┐\n   147→│         │            Use-Case           │\n   148→│         │   (Application Services)      │\n   149→│         └──────────────▲────────────────┘\n   150→│                        │\n   151→│         ┌──────────────┴────────────────┐\n   152→│         │      Enterprise Business      │\n   153→│         │         Rules (Core)          │\n   154→│         └──────────────▲────────────────┘\n   155→│                        │\n   156→│         ┌──────────────┴────────────────┐\n   157→└─DB──────┤  Frameworks & Drivers Layer   │\n   158→  MQ──────┤  (Postgres, RabbitMQ, Redis)  │\n   159→          └───────────────────────────────┘\n   160→```\n   161→\n   162→The **dependency rule** is enforced with compiler firewalls and automated CI checks.\n   163→\n   164→---\n   165→\n   166→## Build Matrix\n   167→\n   168→| Target              | CI Profile            | Sanitizers       |\n   169→| ------------------- | --------------------- | ---------------- |\n   170→| `x86_64-linux-gnu`  | Ubuntu 22.04 (gcc)    | ASan, UBSan      |\n   171→| `arm64-darwin`      | macOS 14 (clang)      | —                |\n   172→| `rpi-armhf`         | Raspbian (cross-gcc)  | —                |\n   173→| `wasm32-wasi`       | WASI-SDK 20           | —                |\n   174→\n   175→---\n   176→\n   177→## Security Posture\n   178→\n   179→EduPay embraces **Security-by-Design**:\n   180→\n   181→* Secrets never live in code—`EP_SECRET_*` env-vars or HashiCorp Vault only.  \n   182→* Mandatory static analysis (clang-tidy, cppcheck) + OSS-Fuzz integration.  \n   183→* Strict compiler flags: `-fstack-protector-strong -fPIE -pie -D_FORTIFY_SOURCE=3`.  \n   184→* Cryptography via [liboqs](https://openquantumsafe.org/) (post-quantum ready).\n   185→\n   186→---\n   187→\n   188→## Testing\n   189→\n   190→```bash\n   191→cmake --build build --target check      # unit + integration\n   192→ctest --output-on-failure\n   193→\n   194→# Property-based fuzzing\n   195→make -C tests/fuzz run\n   196→```\n   197→\n   198→Coverage reports (lcov) are uploaded to Codecov on every push.\n   199→\n   200→---\n   201→\n   202→## Course Integration\n   203→\n   204→Professors: see `docs/curriculum/*.md` for semester outlines.  \n   205→Assignments map to git tags (`v2024s1-lab1`, `v2024s1-lab2`), enabling *git bisect*-based scavenger hunts.\n   206→\n   207→---\n   208→\n Professors may replace **Kafka** with **NATS** or\n    39→**RabbitMQ** without touching business rules.\n    40→\n    41→---\n    42→\n    43→## 3  Clean Architecture Layering\n    44→\n    45→| Layer (Dir) | Stability | Example Components | Compile-Time Dependency |\n    46→|-------------|-----------|--------------------|-------------------------|\n    47→| `domain` | 💎 Most stable | `payment.h`, `currency.h` | _None_ |\n    48→| `usecase` | 🏛️ Stable | `register_student.c`, `process_refund.c` | `domain` |\n    49→| `interface` | 🌐 Replaceable | `http_controller.c`, `grpc_adapter.c` | `usecase` |\n    50→| `infrastructure` | 🔌 Volatile | `postgres_repo.c`, `kafka_producer.c` | `interface` |\n    51→\n    52→The arrows always point _inwards_; outer layers know inner layers, never\n    53→vice-versa.\n    54→\n    55→---\n    56→\n    57→## 4  Bounded Contexts\n    58→\n    59→### 4.1 Admissions\n    60→* Handles enrollment fees and acceptance deposits\n    61→* Emits `AdmissionAccepted` → triggers **Financial-Aid** scholarship allocation saga\n    62→\n    63→### 4.2 Bursar\n    64→* Tuition invoicing, late-fee calculations\n    65→* Maintains **Multi-Currency** tables using ISO-4217\n    66→\n    67→### 4.3 Financial-Aid\n    68→* Scholarship disbursement & stipend scheduling\n    69→* Implements **Fraud Detection** heuristics (`aid_risk_score.c`)\n    70→\n    71→### 4.4 Continuing-Education\n    72→* Pay-per-micro-credential flows & coupon codes\n    73→* Demonstrates **PSD2 SCA** (Strong Customer Authentication) via WebAuthn\n    74→\n    75→---\n    76→\n    77→## 5  Payment Processing Flow (Happy Path)\n    78→\n    79→```plaintext\n    80→┌──────────────────┐\n    81→│ Student Portal   │\n    82→└────────┬─────────┘\n    83→         │ POST /tuition/pay\n    84→         ▼\n    85→┌──────────────────┐\n    86→│ API Gateway      │\n    87→└────────┬─────────┘\n    88→         │ Command: PayTuition\n    89→         ▼\n    90→┌──────────────────┐\n    91→│ Bursar.CmdSvc    │ ① Validate → ② Authorize Card (PCI-DSS) → ③ Emit Paid Event\n    92→└────────┬─────────┘\n    93→         ▼ publish\n    94→┌──────────────────┐\n    95→│ Kafka: TuitionPaid│\n    96→└────────┬─────────┘\n    97→         ▼\n    98→┌──────────────────┐\n    99→│ QuerySvc         │ ④ Update Materialized View\n   100→└──────────────────┘\n   101→```\n   102→\n   103→Error cases (insufficient funds, 3-D Secure failure) are handled by the\n   104→**Saga Orchestrator** which issues compensating transactions.\n   105→\n   106→---\n   107→\n   108→## 6  Saga Pattern Demonstration Mode\n   109→\n   110→| Failure Point | Compensating Action | Teaching Objective |\n   111→|---------------|--------------------|--------------------|\n   112→| FX conversion timeout | Revert ledger entry | Idempotent rollbacks |\n   113→| Scholarship ledger update fails | Re-credit student card | Distributed tracing |\n   114→\n   115→The mode can be toggled via the compile-time flag  \n   116→```c\n   117→#define SAGA_DEMO 1\n   118→```\n   119→in `config/build_flags.h`.\n   120→\n   121→---\n   122→\n   123→## 7  Event Sourcing & CQRS\n   124→\n   125→* **Write Model** – append-only events stored in `audit/ledger.dat`\n   126→* **Read Model** – projections in **PostgreSQL** (can swap with **SQLite** for\n   127→  classroom labs)\n   128→* Replay tool: `bin/ledger-replay --from 2022-01-01`\n   129→\n   130→---\n   131→\n   132→## 8  Security by Design\n   133→\n   134→1. Memory-safe wrappers (`secure_mem.c`) protect against buffer overflows.\n   135→2. PCI-DSS scope is minimized by tokenizing PANs via external vault.\n   136→3. FERPA compliance: PII fields encrypted with **AES-256-GCM**; keys rotated\n   137→   through **HashiCorp Vault** every 24 h.\n   138→4. Static analysis integrated with **clang-tidy** and **CodeQL**.\n   139→\n   140→---\n   141→\n   142→## 9  Extensibility: Swapping a Repository\n   143→\n   144→Example: Replace PostgreSQL with MongoDB in a lab.\n   145→\n   146→1. Implement `mongo_payment_repo.c` conforming to\n   147→   ```c\n   148→   struct payment_repo_vtbl { ... };\n   149→   ```\n   150→2. Register the concrete type in `infra/repo_factory.c`.\n   151→3. No changes required in `usecase/process_payment.c`  \n   152→   thanks to the **Dependency Inversion** rule.\n   153→\n   154→---\n   155→\n   156→## 10  Development Conventions\n   157→\n   158→* **C17** standard, compiled with `-Wall -Wextra -pedantic`.\n   159→* One public header per component; internal helpers stay in `*.c`.\n   160→* Unit tests with **Unity**; acceptance tests scripted in **Bats**.\n   161→* Commit messages follow Conventional Commits:\n   162→  `feat(bursar): add multi-currency rounding bugfix`.\n   163→\n   164→---\n   165→\n   166→## 11  Directory Tree (excerpt)\n   167→\n   168→```plaintext\n   169→src/\n   170→ ├─ admissions/\n   171→ │   ├─ domain/\n   172→ │   ├─ usecase/\n   173→ │   └─ interface/\n   174→ ├─ bursar/\n   175→ │   └─ ...\n   176→ ├─ common/\n   177→ │   ├─ secure_mem.c\n   178→ │   ├─ currency.c\n   179→ │   └─ ...\n   180→docs/\n   181→ └─ architecture/\n   182→     └─ README.md  ← you are here\n   183→tests/\n   184→ ├─ unit/\n   185→ └─ integration/\n   186→```\n   187→\n   188→---\n   189→\n   190→## 12  Future Work\n   191→\n   192→* Integrate OpenTelemetry for distributed tracing labs.\n   193→* Add Rust FFI bindings to compare memory safety.\n   194→* Implement ISO-20022 `pain.001` export for cross-border wires.\n   195→\n   196→---\n   197→\n   198→© 2023 EduPay Ledger Academy.  \n   199→Licensed under the MIT License – see `LICENSE` for details.\n   200→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012EjAPNZD1THyuirnt4qxRC","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→<!--\n     2→EduPay Ledger Academy  \n     3→docs/architecture/02_microservices_and_bounded_contexts.md\n     4→\n     5→This document is part of the executable documentation strategy: every\n     6→code fragment has been extracted from a working test-suite and can be\n     7→compiled in isolation.  When learners copy/paste a snippet they get a\n     8→runnable artefact—not pseudo-code.  (clang ‑std=c17 ‑Wall ‑Wextra\n     9→-pedantic ‑O2 listing.c ‑o listing && ./listing)\n    10→-->\n    11→\n    12→# Micro-services & Bounded Contexts\n    13→\n    14→EduPay Ledger Academy follows Domain-Driven Design and splits the\n    15→business domain into **four bounded contexts** (BCs).  Each BC is\n    16→implemented as one or more micro-services—small autonomous executables\n    17→written in C and packaged as OCI containers.\n    18→\n    19→| Bounded Context         | Canonical Service | Purpose                                                                    |\n    20→|-------------------------|-------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n    21→| Admissions              | `admissions-api`  | On-boards students, allocates student-ids, emits *StudentRegistered*       |\n    22→| Bursar (A/R)            | `ledger-core`     | Double-entry ledger, tuition invoicing, GL postings, payment gateway       |\n    23→| Financial-Aid           | `aid-engine`      | Award rules, stipend disbursement, compliance (FERPA / PSD2 / PCI-DSS)     |\n    24→| Continuing-Education    | `micro-cred`      | Per-course micro-credential tracking, pay-per-module split-payments         |\n    25→\n    26→A **Shared-Kernel** provides value-objects (`Money`, `Currency`,\n    27→`StudentId`) and the **AuditTrail** event store.\n    28→\n    29→```\n    30→                        +-----------+\n    31→                        |  API-GW   |\n    32→                        +-----------+\n    33→                              |\n    34→            +-----------------+------------------+\n    35→            |                 |                  |\n    36→    +--------------+  +---------------+  +---------------+\n    37→    | admissions   |  | ledger-core   |  | aid-engine    |\n    38→    +--------------+  +---------------+  +---------------+\n    39→            \\           /       \\              /\n    40→             \\         /         \\            /\n    41→              \\       /           \\          /\n    42→              +----- Event Bus (NATS / Kafka) -+\n    43→                        ^\n    44→                        |\n    45→                 +---------------+\n    46→                 |  AuditTrail   |\n    47→                 +---------------+\n    48→```\n    49→\n    50→## Event Catalog (Ubiquitous Language)\n    51→\n    52→* `StudentRegistered`\n    53→* `InvoiceRaised`\n    54→* `PaymentAuthorized`\n    55→* `AidDisbursed`\n    56→* `LedgerEntryPosted`\n    57→* `SagaCompensationRequired`\n    58→\n    59→### Example Domain Event (C, Protobuf-ish Header)\n    60→\n    61→```c\n    62→/* events/payment_authorized.h */\n    63→#ifndef EP_EVENTS_PAYMENT_AUTH_H\n    64→#define EP_EVENTS_PAYMENT_AUTH_H\n    65→/*\n    66→  Domain Event: PaymentAuthorized\n    67→  This event is emitted by ledger-core after the acquiring bank\n    68→  responds with an authorization code.  Other contexts (Financial-Aid,\n    69→  Continuing-Education) subscribe to project read models.\n    70→*/\n    71→#include <stdint.h>\n    72→#include <time.h>\n    73→\n    74→#define PAYMENT_AUTH_VERSION 1\n    75→\n    76→typedef struct ep_payment_auth_t {\n    77→    uint8_t  version;          /* schema version                     */\n    78→    char     id[37];           /* UUID v4 string                     */\n    79→    char     student_id[16];   /* canonical SID                      */\n    80→    char     invoice_id[37];   /* payment belongs to which invoice   */\n    81→    char     currency[4];      /* ISO-4217                           */\n    82→    int64_t  amount_minor;     /* 1099 = $10.99 ( if cents )         */\n    83→    char     auth_code[12];    /* provided by payment processor      */\n    84→    time_t   occurred_at_utc;  /* RFC 3339 ts                        */\n    85→} ep_payment_auth_t;\n    86→\n    87→#endif /* EP_EVENTS_PAYMENT_AUTH_H */\n    88→```\n    89→\n    90→## Service Skeleton: `ledger-core`\n    91→\n    92→The canonical micro-service exposes gRPC, publishes events on NATS,\n    93→and persists to PostgreSQL.  Internally, Clean Architecture layers are\n    94→segregated into `domain`, `usecase`, and `infra` directories.\n    95→\n    96→### `domain/money.h`\n    97→\n    98→```c\n    99→/* domain/money.h */\n   100→#ifndef EP_DOMAIN_MONEY_H\n   101→#define EP_DOMAIN_MONEY_H\n   102→\n   103→#include <stdint.h>\n   104→\n   105→typedef struct ep_money_t {\n   106→    int64_t amount_minor;  /* -922337203685477 */\n   107→    char    currency[4];   /* \"USD\"            */\n   108→} ep_money_t;\n   109→\n   110→/* Equality check without floating-point errors */\n   111→static inline int ep_money_eq(const ep_money_t *a, const ep_money_t *b)\n   112→{\n   113→    return a && b &&\n   114→           a->amount_minor == b->amount_minor &&\n   115→           a->currency[0] == b->currency[0] &&\n   116→           a->currency[1] == b->currency[1] &&\n   117→           a->currency[2] == b->currency[2] &&\n   118→           a->currency[3] == b->currency[3];\n   119→}\n   120→\n   121→#endif /* EP_DOMAIN_MONEY_H */\n   122→```\n   123→\n   124→### `usecase/post_payment.c`\n   125→\n   126→```c\n   127→/* usecase/post_payment.c */\n   128→#include \"domain/money.h\"\n   129→#include \"events/payment_authorized.h\"\n   130→#include <stdio.h>\n   131→#include <string.h>\n   132→\n   133→typedef struct ep_payment_repo ep_payment_repo_t;\n   134→typedef struct ep_event_bus   ep_event_bus_t;\n   135→\n   136→/* Dependency inversion via pure C interfaces */\n   137→struct ep_payment_repo {\n   138→    int (*begin_tx)(ep_payment_repo_t *);\n   139→    int (*commit_tx)(ep_payment_repo_t *);\n   140→    int (*rollback_tx)(ep_payment_repo_t *);\n   141→    int (*insert_ledger_entry)(ep_payment_repo_t *,\n   142→                               const char  *invoice_id,\n   143→                               const ep_money_t *amount,\n   144→                               const char  *auth_code);\n   145→};\n   146→\n   147→struct ep_event_bus {\n   148→    int (*publish)(ep_event_bus_t *, const void *evt, size_t sz);\n   149→};\n   150→\n   151→/*\n   152→  Post a payment to the double-entry ledger.\n   153→\n   154→  Returns 0 on success, < 0 on domain error, > 0 on infra error.\n   155→*/\n   156→int ep_uc_post_payment(ep_payment_repo_t *repo,\n   157→                       ep_event_bus_t    *bus,\n   158→                       const char        *invoice_id,\n   159→                       const ep_money_t  *amount,\n   160→                       const char        *auth_code)\n   161→{\n   162→    if (!repo || !bus || !invoice_id || !amount || !auth_code)\n   163→        return -1; /* pre-condition failed */\n   164→\n   165→    if (repo->begin_tx(repo) != 0)\n   166→        return  1; /* infra error */\n   167→\n   168→    if (repo->insert_ledger_entry(repo, invoice_id, amount, auth_code) != 0) {\n   169→        repo->rollback_tx(repo);\n   170→        return  2;\n   171→    }\n   172→\n   173→    ep_payment_auth_t evt = {\n   174→        .version        = PAYMENT_AUTH_VERSION,\n   175→        .occurred_at_utc= time(NULL),\n   176→    };\n   177→    /* (Safe) string copy utilities omitted for brevity */\n   178→    strncpy(evt.id,        \"UUID-GEN-STUB\", sizeof evt.id);\n   179→    strncpy(evt.student_id,\"SID-LOOKUP-X\", sizeof evt.student_id);\n   180→    strncpy(evt.invoice_id,invoice_id,     sizeof evt.invoice_id);\n   181→    strncpy(evt.currency,  amount->currency, sizeof evt.currency);\n   182→    evt.amount_minor = amount->amount_minor;\n   183→    strncpy(evt.auth_code, auth_code, sizeof evt.auth_code);\n   184→\n   185→    if (bus->publish(bus, &evt, sizeof evt) != 0) {\n   186→        repo->rollback_tx(repo);\n   187→        return  3;\n   188→    }\n   189→\n   190→    return repo->commit_tx(repo);\n   191→}\n   192→```\n   193→\n   194→### Saga Orchestrator (Distributed Tuition Payment)\n   195→\n   196→```c\n   197→/* saga/tuition_payment_saga.h */\n   198→#ifndef EP_SAGA_TUITION_PAYMENT_H\n   199→#define EP_SAGA_TUITION_PAYMENT_H\n   200→\n   201→/*\n   202→  TuitionPaymentSaga orchestrates:\n   203→\n   204→  1. Reserve Financial-Aid budget\n   205→  2. Authorize card payment (Bursar)\n   206→  3. Disburse stipends\n   207→  4. Issue micro-credential revenue split\n   208→\n   209→  Compensation order is reverse.\n   210→\n   211→  State machine is stored in the EventStore; students step through\n   212→  failures during outage simulations.\n   213→*/\n   214→\n   215→typedef enum {\n   216→    SAGA_STEP_NONE = 0,\n   217→    SAGA_STEP_AID_RESERVED,\n   218→    SAGA_STEP_PAYMENT_AUTHORIZED,\n   219→    SAGA_STEP_STIPEND_PAID,\n   220→    SAGA_STEP_MICROCRED_SPLIT,\n   221→    SAGA_STEP_DONE,\n   222→    SAGA_STEP_FAILED\n   223→} ep_saga_state_t;\n   224→\n   225→#endif /* EP_SAGA_TUITION_PAYMENT_H */\n   226→```\n   227→\n   228→## Anti-corruption Layer (ACL) Sample\n   229→\n   230→Admissions uses an external vendor for SIS and must translate its JSON\n   231→schema into *StudentRegistered* events.\n   232→\n   233→```c\n   234→/* acl/sis_adapter.c */\n   235→#include <jansson.h>\n   236→#include \"events/student_registered.h\"\n   237→\n   238→/* Returns 0 on success */\n   239→int ep_acl_translate_sis_event(const char *json,\n   240→                               ep_student_reg_t *out_evt)\n   241→{\n   242→    json_error_t err;\n   243→    json_t *root = json_loads(json, 0, &err);\n   244→    if (!root) return -1;\n   245→\n   246→    const char *sid   = json_string_value(json_object_get(root, \"sid\"));\n   247→    const char *email = json_string_value(json_object_get(root, \"email\"));\n   248→    if (!sid || !email) {\n   249→        json_decref(root);\n   250→        return -2;\n   251→    }\n   252→\n   253→    strncpy(out_evt->student_id, sid, sizeof out_evt->student_id);\n   254→    strncpy(out_evt->email,      email, sizeof out_evt->email);\n   255→    out_evt->occurred_at_utc = time(NULL);\n   256→\n   257→    json_decref(root);\n   258→    return 0;\n   259→}\n   260→```\n   261→\n   262→## Data Ownership & ACID Boundaries\n   263→\n   264→* **Admissions** owns `Student` aggregate; others reference via SID.\n   265→* **Ledger-core** owns `Invoice`, `LedgerEntry`.\n   266→* **Financial-Aid** owns `Award`, `AidDisbursement`.\n   267→* Shared Kernel’s `AuditTrail` is append-only; no service can mutate.\n   268→\n   269→| Service        | DB Schema          | Tx Boundary (Local ACID)    |\n   270→|----------------|--------------------|-----------------------------|\n   271→| admissions     | `admissions.*`     | `Student`, `Applicant`      |\n   272→| ledger-core    | `ledger.*`         | `Invoice`, `LedgerEntry`    |\n   273→| aid-engine     | `aid.*`            | `Award`, `Disbursement`     |\n   274→| micro-cred     | `microcred.*`      | `CourseRun`, `SplitPayout`  |\n   275→\n   276→## Resilience Policy\n   277→\n   278→1. Idempotent command handlers.\n   279→2. At-least-once event delivery (NATS JetStream).\n   280→3. Circuit-breaker around external PSP, open after 3 failures / 30 s.\n   281→4. Saga compensation for inter-context failure.\n   282→\n   283→```c\n   284→/* infra/circuit_breaker.h */\n   285→#ifndef EP_INFRA_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_H\n   286→#define EP_INFRA_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_H\n   287→#include <time.h>\n   288→typedef enum { CB_CLOSED, CB_OPEN, CB_HALF_OPEN } cb_state_t;\n   289→\n   290→typedef struct circuit_breaker {\n   291→    cb_state_t state;\n   292→    unsigned   failure_count;\n   293→    time_t     last_failure;\n   294→    unsigned   open_timeout_sec;\n   295→    unsigned   threshold;\n   296→} circuit_breaker_t;\n   297→\n   298→void cb_init(circuit_breaker_t *cb,\n   299→             unsigned threshold,\n   300→             unsigned open_timeout_sec);\n   301→\n   302→int  cb_before_request(circuit_breaker_t *cb);\n   303→void cb_after_success(circuit_breaker_t *cb);\n   304→void cb_after_failure(circuit_breaker_t *cb);\n   305→\n   306→#endif /* EP_INFRA_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_H */\n   307→```\n   308→\n   309→## Build & Deployment Standards\n   310→\n   311→* `Dockerfile` uses scratch + `musl-clang` static linking ⇒ 6 MB images.\n   312→* Each service exports `/livez` and `/readyz` over HTTP on port 8080.\n   313→* Kubernetes Helm charts pin CPU/memory, configure HPA on NATS lag.\n   314→\n   315→---\n   316→\n   317→> “Architecture is about the important stuff—whatever that is.”  \n   318→> — Ralph Johnson\n   319→\n   320→Learners should fork this repository, enable **Saga Pattern\n   321→Demonstration Mode** (`make chaos-on`), and trigger the `tuition\n   322→checkout` flow.  When the intentional fault in step 2 forces a\n   323→rollback, open the **AuditTrail dashboard** and correlate the saga\n   324→compensating transactions.\n   325→\n   326→Happy hacking!\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01B7UML1suop5DdcU5myYWwL","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→---\n     3→title: EduPay Ledger Academy – CQRS & Event Sourcing Architecture\n     4→description: Technical deep-dive into how the EduPay Ledger Academy executes Command-Query Responsibility Segregation (CQRS) and Event Sourcing in C while remaining faithful to Clean Architecture.\n     5→slug: /architecture/cqrs-and-event-sourcing\n     6→---\n     7→\n     8→# 03 – CQRS & Event Sourcing\n     9→\n    10→> “Separate **what** the system does from **what** the system knows.”  \n    11→> — EduPay Core Team\n    12→\n    13→EduPay Ledger Academy employs **CQRS** to divide **mutations** (Commands) from **reads** (Queries) so that educators can swap persistence technologies, message buses, or reporting databases without touching domain logic.   \n    14→All state-changing requests are persisted as **immutable domain events**, enabling deterministic replay, powerful audit trails, and student-friendly time-travel debugging.\n    15→\n    16→---\n    17→\n    18→## Table of Contents\n    19→\n    20→1. Domain Vocabulary  \n    21→2. High Level Flow Diagram  \n    22→3. Command Pipeline (Write Model)  \n    23→4. Event Store Implementation (C)  \n    24→5. Query Model & Projections  \n    25→6. Consistency, Concurrency, and Idempotency  \n    26→7. Failure Injection Labs  \n    27→8. Further Reading\n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## 1. Domain Vocabulary\n    32→\n    33→Term | Definition\n    34→---- | ----------\n    35→**Command** | An intent _to do_ something. Must either succeed or return an error.\n    36→**Event** | A fact _that something happened_. Can never change or be removed.\n    37→**Aggregate** | A transactional consistency boundary, e.g., `LedgerAccount`.\n    38→**Projection / Read Model** | A denormalized view optimized for reads: dashboards, ledgers, etc.\n    39→\n    40→---\n    41→\n    42→## 2. High Level Flow Diagram\n    43→\n    44→```mermaid\n    45→sequenceDiagram\n    46→    actor Student\n    47→    participant CMD as Command API\n    48→    participant CH as Command Handler\n    49→    participant ES as Event Store (append-only)\n    50→    participant PR as Projector\n    51→    participant RM as Read Model (SQLite)\n    52→    Student->>CMD: POST /accounts/deposit\n    53→    CMD->>CH: DepositFundsCommand\n    54→    CH->>ES: Append(EventDeposited)\n    55→    ES->>PR: Emit(EventDeposited)\n    56→    PR->>RM: UPDATE balances\n    57→    Student->>RM: GET /dashboard\n    58→```\n    59→\n    60→---\n    61→\n    62→## 3. Command Pipeline (Write Model)\n    63→\n    64→Below is a **compile-ready** excerpt (C17) showing how a `CommandBus` validates and dispatches commands to their handlers.  \n    65→It purposefully decouples IO so professors can mock the bus during unit tests.\n    66→\n    67→```c\n    68→/* File: src/cqrs/command_bus.h */\n    69→#ifndef EDU_PAY_COMMAND_BUS_H\n    70→#define EDU_PAY_COMMAND_BUS_H\n    71→\n    72→#include <stdint.h>\n    73→#include <stddef.h>\n    74→#include \"utils/uuid.h\"\n    75→\n    76→/* ---------- Generic Command Definition ---------- */\n    77→typedef struct {\n    78→    uuid_t   id;         /* Correlation ID for tracing */\n    79→    uint64_t timestamp;  /* epoch millis (monotonic)   */\n    80→    const char *type;    /* \"DepositFundsCommand\" etc  */\n    81→    void     *payload;   /* domain-specific struct ptr */\n    82→} command_t;\n    83→\n    84→/* Error codes follow POSIX semantics for reuse in shell scripts */\n    85→typedef enum {\n    86→    CMD_OK          = 0,\n    87→    CMD_ERR_INPUT   = 22,  /* EINVAL */\n    88→    CMD_ERR_HANDLER = 38,  /* ENOSYS */\n    89→    CMD_ERR_REJECT  = 35   /* EDEADLK – business rule violated */\n    90→} command_status_e;\n    91→\n    92→/* Opaque route handle resolves to specific handler at runtime */\n    93→typedef command_status_e (*command_handler_f)(const command_t *cmd);\n    94→\n    95→/* Register handler */\n    96→int command_bus_register(const char *type, command_handler_f fn);\n    97→\n    98→/* Dispatch (synchronous) */\n    99→command_status_e command_bus_execute(const command_t *cmd);\n   100→\n   101→#endif /* EDU_PAY_COMMAND_BUS_H */\n   102→```\n   103→\n   104→```c\n   105→/* File: src/cqrs/command_bus.c */\n   106→#include \"cqrs/command_bus.h\"\n   107→#include <stdlib.h>\n   108→#include <string.h>\n   109→#include \"utils/hashtable.h\"\n   110→\n   111→/* --- Very light associative table (string → function ptr) --- */\n   112→static hash_table_t *router = NULL;\n   113→\n   114→int command_bus_register(const char *type, command_handler_f fn) {\n   115→    if (!type || !fn) return -CMD_ERR_INPUT;\n   116→    if (!router) router = ht_create(32);\n   117→    return ht_put(router, type, fn);\n   118→}\n   119→\n   120→command_status_e command_bus_execute(const command_t *cmd) {\n   121→    if (!cmd || !cmd->type) return CMD_ERR_INPUT;\n   122→    command_handler_f fn = ht_get(router, cmd->type);\n   123→    if (!fn) return CMD_ERR_HANDLER;\n   124→    return fn(cmd);\n   125→}\n   126→```\n   127→\n   128→Example Domain Command + Handler:\n   129→\n   130→```c\n   131→/* File: src/payments/commands/deposit_funds.h */\n   132→#ifndef EDU_PAY_COMMAND_DEPOSIT_H\n   133→#define EDU_PAY_COMMAND_DEPOSIT_H\n   134→#include <stdint.h>\n   135→\n   136→typedef struct {\n   137→    char     account_id[36];\n   138→    uint64_t amount_cents;\n   139→    char     currency[4];        /* \"USD\" | \"EUR\" | etc. */\n   140→} deposit_funds_payload_t;\n   141→\n   142→#endif\n   143→```\n   144→\n   145→```c\n   146→/* File: src/payments/handlers/deposit_funds_handler.c */\n   147→#include \"cqrs/command_bus.h\"\n   148→#include \"events/event_bus.h\"\n   149→#include \"payments/commands/deposit_funds.h\"\n   150→#include \"payments/events/funds_deposited.h\"\n   151→#include \"domain/accounts_repository.h\"\n   152→#include \"utils/clock.h\"\n   153→#include \"utils/currency.h\"\n   154→\n   155→static command_status_e handle_deposit(const command_t *cmd)\n   156→{\n   157→    if (!cmd || !cmd->payload) return CMD_ERR_INPUT;\n   158→    const deposit_funds_payload_t *pl = cmd->payload;\n   159→\n   160→    /* --- Business Rule Enforcement --- */\n   161→    if (!currency_is_supported(pl->currency) || pl->amount_cents == 0)\n   162→        return CMD_ERR_REJECT;\n   163→\n   164→    /* Optimistic lock fetch */\n   165→    ledger_account_t acct;\n   166→    if (accounts_repo_load(pl->account_id, &acct) != 0)\n   167→        return CMD_ERR_REJECT; /* Unknown account */\n   168→\n   169→    /* Domain invariants can run here (e.g., AML limits) */\n   170→    /* ... */\n   171→\n   172→    /* --- Produce Event (immutably) --- */\n   173→    funds_deposited_event_t ev = {\n   174→        .base.id        = uuid_v4(),\n   175→        .base.timestamp = clock_epoch_ms(),\n   176→        .base.type      = \"FundsDepositedEvent\",\n   177→        .account_id     = {0},\n   178→        .amount_cents   = pl->amount_cents,\n   179→        .currency       = {0}\n   180→    };\n   181→    strncpy(ev.account_id, pl->account_id, sizeof(ev.account_id)-1);\n   182→    strncpy(ev.currency, pl->currency,   sizeof(ev.currency)-1);\n   183→\n   184→    /* Persist via Event Bus (which writes through to Event Store) */\n   185→    if (event_bus_publish((event_t *)&ev) != EVT_OK)\n   186→        return CMD_ERR_HANDLER;\n   187→\n   188→    return CMD_OK;\n   189→}\n   190→\n   191→/* Auto-register at startup */\n   192→__attribute__((constructor))\n   193→static void _deposit_register(void) {\n   194→    command_bus_register(\"DepositFundsCommand\", handle_deposit);\n   195→}\n   196→```\n   197→\n   198→---\n   199→\n   200→## 4. Event Store Implementation (C)\n   201→\n   202→The **Event Store** is append-only, leverages `mmap(2)` for re-play speed, and stores each record as length-prefixed JSON so that students can grep logs with *jq*.\n   203→\n   204→```c\n   205→/* File: src/event_store/event_store.h */\n   206→#ifndef EDU_PAY_EVENT_STORE_H\n   207→#define EDU_PAY_EVENT_STORE_H\n   208→\n   209→#include <stddef.h>\n   210→#include <stdint.h>\n   211→#include \"events/event.h\"\n   212→\n   213→#define EVT_STORE_OK          0\n   214→#define EVT_STORE_ERR_IO    (-5)\n   215→#define EVT_STORE_ERR_FULL (-28)    /* ENOSPC */\n   216→\n   217→/* Append single event (thread-safe, fsync’ed) */\n   218→int event_store_append(const event_t *ev);\n   219→\n   220→/* Stream all events for replay (blocking). \n   221→   Calls `callback` for each event; stop when it returns non-zero. */\n   222→typedef int (*event_stream_cb)(const event_t *ev, void *ctx);\n   223→int event_store_stream(event_stream_cb callback, void *ctx);\n   224→\n   225→#endif\n   226→```\n   227→\n   228→```c\n   229→/* File: src/event_store/event_store.c */\n   230→#define _POSIX_C_SOURCE 200809L\n   231→#include \"event_store/event_store.h\"\n   232→#include <fcntl.h>\n   233→#include <sys/mman.h>\n   234→#include <sys/stat.h>\n   235→#include <unistd.h>\n   236→#include <pthread.h>\n   237→#include <stdio.h>\n   238→#include <string.h>\n   239→#include \"utils/serialize_json.h\"\n   240→#include \"utils/spinlock.h\"\n   241→\n   242→#define EVENT_STORE_FILE \"./var/event_store.log\"\n   243→#define MAX_EVENT_SIZE   4096   /* hard cap to aid undergrad sanity */\n   244→\n   245→static int fd = -1;\n   246→static pthread_spinlock_t lock;\n   247→\n   248→__attribute__((constructor))\n   249→static void _init_store(void) {\n   250→    fd = open(EVENT_STORE_FILE, O_RDWR | O_CREAT | O_APPEND, 0640);\n   251→    pthread_spin_init(&lock, PTHREAD_PROCESS_PRIVATE);\n   252→}\n   253→\n   254→__attribute__((destructor))\n   255→static void _close_store(void) {\n   256→    if (fd != -1) close(fd);\n   257→    pthread_spin_destroy(&lock);\n   258→}\n   259→\n   260→int event_store_append(const event_t *ev)\n   261→{\n   262→    if (!ev) return EVT_STORE_ERR_IO;\n   263→\n   264→    char buffer[MAX_EVENT_SIZE];\n   265→    ssize_t len = json_serialize_event(ev, buffer, sizeof(buffer));\n   266→    if (len < 0) return EVT_STORE_ERR_IO;\n   267→\n   268→    /* Length-prefixed frame */\n   269→    uint32_t nlen = (uint32_t)len;\n   270→    pthread_spin_lock(&lock);\n   271→    ssize_t w = write(fd, &nlen, sizeof(nlen));\n   272→    w += write(fd, buffer, len);\n   273→    fsync(fd);\n   274→    pthread_spin_unlock(&lock);\n   275→\n   276→    return (w == (ssize_t)(sizeof(nlen) + len)) ? EVT_STORE_OK\n   277→                                                : EVT_STORE_ERR_IO;\n   278→}\n   279→\n   280→int event_store_stream(event_stream_cb cb, void *ctx)\n   281→{\n   282→    if (!cb) return EVT_STORE_ERR_IO;\n   283→\n   284→    int sfd = open(EVENT_STORE_FILE, O_RDONLY);\n   285→    if (sfd < 0) return EVT_STORE_ERR_IO;\n   286→\n   287→    uint32_t nlen;\n   288→    while (read(sfd, &nlen, sizeof(nlen)) == sizeof(nlen)) {\n   289→        if (nlen > MAX_EVENT_SIZE) { close(sfd); return EVT_STORE_ERR_IO; }\n   290→        char buffer[MAX_EVENT_SIZE];\n   291→        if (read(sfd, buffer, nlen) != (ssize_t)nlen) break;\n   292→\n   293→        event_t *ev = json_deserialize_event(buffer, nlen);\n   294→        if (!ev) continue;\n   295→        if (cb(ev, ctx) != 0) { free(ev); break; }\n   296→        free(ev);\n   297→    }\n   298→    close(sfd);\n   299→    return EVT_STORE_OK;\n   300→}\n   301→```\n   302→\n   303→---\n   304→\n   305→## 5. Query Model & Projections\n   306→\n   307→Read models are **eventually consistent**. Every event published above is fanned-out to one or more *Projectors* that transform it into query-optimized databases (SQLite, Redis, CSV for Excel labs).\n   308→\n   309→```c\n   310→/* File: src/projections/balance_projection.c */\n   311→#include \"events/event_bus.h\"\n   312→#include \"payments/events/funds_deposited.h\"\n   313→#include \"database/sqlite.h\"\n   314→\n   315→static int on_funds_deposited(const event_t *ev)\n   316→{\n   317→    const funds_deposited_event_t *e = (const funds_deposited_event_t *)ev;\n   318→    return sqlite_exec(\n   319→        \"INSERT INTO account_balance(account_id, currency, balance_cents) \"\n   320→        \"VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3) \"\n   321→        \"ON CONFLICT(account_id, currency) \"\n   322→        \"DO UPDATE SET balance_cents = balance_cents + excluded.balance_cents;\",\n   323→        e->account_id, e->currency, (int64_t)e->amount_cents);\n   324→}\n   325→\n   326→__attribute__((constructor))\n   327→static void _register_projector(void) {\n   328→    event_bus_subscribe(\"FundsDepositedEvent\", on_funds_deposited);\n   329→}\n   330→```\n   331→\n   332→Students can query via the REST shell or `sqlite3 var/read_models.db`.\n   333→\n   334→---\n   335→\n   336→## 6. Consistency, Concurrency, and Idempotency\n   337→\n   338→• **Concurrency Control** – Each aggregate carries a monotonically increasing `version`. Handlers pass the expected `version` into `event_store_append()`.  \n   339→• **At-Least-Once Delivery** – Projectors keep an `offset` table so they can re-play events idempotently.  \n   340→• **Snap-Shots** – Long-lived aggregates (>2 000 events) periodically persist snapshots to avoid hot-loop rehydration in class.\n   341→\n   342→---\n   343→\n   344→## 7. Failure Injection Labs\n   345→\n   346→Activate with:\n   347→\n   348→```bash\n   349→# Simulate projector downtime\n   350→$ make simulate_projector_outage DURATION=30s\n   351→\n   352→# Induce write-model crash mid-transaction\n   353→$ make chaos_saga MODULE=payments\n   354→```\n   355→\n   356→Students then debug replay & compensating actions per the **Saga Pattern**.\n   357→\n   358→---\n   359→\n   360→## 8. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01G4ebWtFU9eEdgSAFe8dkuZ","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# EduPay Ledger Academy  \n     3→### docs/architecture/04_saga_pattern.md\n     4→---\n     5→> Version: 1.3  \n     6→> Last Reviewed: 2024-05-09  \n     7→> Authors: Core Platform Team (@edupay-ledger)  \n     8→> Status: ✅ Production  \n     9→---\n    10→\n    11→## 1. Why EduPay Uses the Saga Pattern\n    12→\n    13→EduPay processes **millions of distributed, financial micro-transactions** each semester.  \n    14→The platform deliberately avoids *single-phase* database transactions because:\n    15→\n    16→* **Micro-services** compose payments from multiple bounded contexts (Admissions, Bursar, Treasury, FX, Fraud).  \n    17→* **Heterogeneous data stores** (PostgreSQL, Redis, EventStoreDB) participate in a single business flow.  \n    18→* **Resiliency labs** in coursework require controlled failure injection and roll-back.  \n    19→\n    20→A **Saga** slices a long-running business process into a series of *local* ACID transactions plus **compensating steps**.  \n    21→If any step fails, the orchestrator triggers rollback actions in **reverse order**, yielding *eventual consistency* without locking global resources.\n    22→\n    23→```\n    24→Enrollment_Fee → FX_Conversion → Treasury_Posting → Fraud_Scoring → Receipt_Email\n    25→         │                │                │              │\n    26→         └────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────┴─────┐\n    27→                       Saga Orchestrator (payment_saga.c)       │\n    28→                       • persists state in Audit_Trail          │\n    29→                       • broadcasts events over EventBus        │\n    30→                       • drives rollbacks on failure ◄──────────┘\n    31→```\n    32→\n    33→---\n    34→\n    35→## 2. High-Level Flow\n    36→\n    37→1. **Create** a `payment_saga_t` record in the `audit_trail` stream.  \n    38→2. **Send** `SAGA_STEP_BEGIN` for *FX_Conversion* (`fx.c`).  \n    39→3. **Local commit** in FX; publish `SAGA_STEP_DONE`.  \n    40→4. Orchestrator continues to *Treasury_Posting* (`ledger.c`).  \n    41→5. If *Fraud_Scoring* fails, **publish** `SAGA_ABORT`.  \n    42→6. Participants receiving `SAGA_ABORT` invoke *compensating* handlers.  \n    43→7. Saga ends with `SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE` or `SAGA_SUCCESS`.\n    44→\n    45→---\n    46→\n    47→## 3. Reference Implementation (C)\n    48→\n    49→All code is kept framework-agnostic, consistent with **Clean Architecture**.  \n    50→Dependencies limited to the standard C library (`pthread` for concurrency).\n    51→\n    52→### 3.1. Public Interface – `include/saga.h`\n    53→\n    54→```c\n    55→#ifndef EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n    56→#define EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n    57→\n    58→#include <stdint.h>\n    59→#include <stdbool.h>\n    60→\n    61→/* ---------- Event Bus ---------------------------------------------------- */\n    62→\n    63→typedef enum {\n    64→    EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN,\n    65→    EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE,\n    66→    EVT_SAGA_ABORT,\n    67→    EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n    68→    EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS\n    69→} saga_event_type_t;\n    70→\n    71→typedef struct {\n    72→    saga_event_type_t type;\n    73→    uint64_t          saga_id;\n    74→    const char       *step_name;   /* e.g., \"FX_Conversion\" */\n    75→    void             *payload;     /* opaque domain state   */\n    76→} saga_event_t;\n    77→\n    78→/* Subscriber callback signature */\n    79→typedef void (*event_handler_f)(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx);\n    80→\n    81→/* Registers a handler; returns 0 on success, -1 on error */\n    82→int eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx);\n    83→\n    84→/* Broadcasts event to all subscribers; thread-safe */\n    85→int eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt);\n    86→\n    87→/* ---------- Saga Orchestrator ------------------------------------------- */\n    88→\n    89→#define SAGA_MAX_STEPS 8\n    90→#define SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX 32\n    91→\n    92→typedef int (*saga_step_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void **payload);\n    93→typedef int (*saga_comp_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void *payload);\n    94→\n    95→typedef struct {\n    96→    char         name[SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX];\n    97→    saga_step_f  action;\n    98→    saga_comp_f  compensate;\n    99→} saga_step_def_t;\n   100→\n   101→typedef struct {\n   102→    uint64_t        id;\n   103→    uint8_t         step_count;\n   104→    uint8_t         current;\n   105→    saga_step_def_t steps[SAGA_MAX_STEPS];\n   106→    bool            aborted;\n   107→} payment_saga_t;\n   108→\n   109→/* Initializes a saga with the supplied steps */\n   110→void saga_init(payment_saga_t *saga, uint64_t id,\n   111→               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n   112→               uint8_t step_count);\n   113→\n   114→/* Executes saga synchronously; returns 0 on success, -1 on failure */\n   115→int saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga);\n   116→\n   117→#endif /* EDUPAY_SAGA_H */\n   118→```\n   119→\n   120→### 3.2. Core Implementation – `src/saga.c`\n   121→\n   122→```c\n   123→#include \"saga.h\"\n   124→#include <pthread.h>\n   125→#include <stdio.h>\n   126→#include <stdlib.h>\n   127→#include <string.h>\n   128→\n   129→#define BUS_MAX_HANDLERS 16\n   130→\n   131→/* ----------------- Simple In-Process Event Bus -------------------------- */\n   132→\n   133→typedef struct {\n   134→    event_handler_f cb;\n   135→    void           *ctx;\n   136→} handler_entry_t;\n   137→\n   138→static handler_entry_t BUS[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\n   139→static pthread_mutex_t BUS_LOCK = PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER;\n   140→\n   141→int eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx)\n   142→{\n   143→    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   144→    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n   145→        if (BUS[i].cb == NULL) {\n   146→            BUS[i].cb  = cb;\n   147→            BUS[i].ctx = ctx;\n   148→            pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   149→            return 0;\n   150→        }\n   151→    }\n   152→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   153→    return -1; /* No slots */\n   154→}\n   155→\n   156→int eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt)\n   157→{\n   158→    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   159→    handler_entry_t handlers[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\n   160→    memcpy(handlers, BUS, sizeof(handlers));\n   161→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   162→\n   163→    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n   164→        if (handlers[i].cb)\n   165→            handlers[i].cb(evt, handlers[i].ctx);\n   166→    }\n   167→    return 0;\n   168→}\n   169→\n   170→/* ----------------- Saga Orchestration ---------------------------------- */\n   171→\n   172→static void publish_step_event(saga_event_type_t type,\n   173→                               const payment_saga_t *saga,\n   174→                               const char *step_name,\n   175→                               void *payload)\n   176→{\n   177→    saga_event_t evt = {\n   178→        .type      = type,\n   179→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   180→        .step_name = step_name,\n   181→        .payload   = payload\n   182→    };\n   183→    eventbus_publish(&evt);\n   184→}\n   185→\n   186→void saga_init(payment_saga_t *s, uint64_t id,\n   187→               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n   188→               uint8_t step_count)\n   189→{\n   190→    memset(s, 0, sizeof(*s));\n   191→    s->id         = id;\n   192→    s->step_count = step_count > SAGA_MAX_STEPS ? SAGA_MAX_STEPS : step_count;\n   193→    memcpy(s->steps, steps, s->step_count * sizeof(saga_step_def_t));\n   194→}\n   195→\n   196→static int rollback(payment_saga_t *saga)\n   197→{\n   198→    for (int i = saga->current; i >= 0; --i) {\n   199→        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[i];\n   200→        if (step->compensate) {\n   201→            publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_ABORT, saga, step->name, NULL);\n   202→            if (step->compensate(saga->id, NULL) != 0)\n   203→                fprintf(stderr, \"⚠  Compensation failed at %s\\n\", step->name);\n   204→        }\n   205→    }\n   206→    saga_event_t done = {\n   207→        .type      = EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n   208→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   209→        .step_name = \"ROLLBACK\"\n   210→    };\n   211→    eventbus_publish(&done);\n   212→    return -1;\n   213→}\n   214→\n   215→int saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga)\n   216→{\n   217→    for (saga->current = 0; saga->current < saga->step_count; ++saga->current) {\n   218→        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[saga->current];\n   219→\n   220→        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN, saga, step->name, NULL);\n   221→\n   222→        void *payload = NULL;\n   223→        if (step->action(saga->id, &payload) != 0) {\n   224→            fprintf(stderr, \"❌  Saga %lu failed at step %s\\n\",\n   225→                    saga->id, step->name);\n   226→            saga->aborted = true;\n   227→            return rollback(saga);\n   228→        }\n   229→\n   230→        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE, saga, step->name, payload);\n   231→    }\n   232→\n   233→    saga_event_t ok = {\n   234→        .type      = EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS,\n   235→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   236→        .step_name = \"COMPLETED\"\n   237→    };\n   238→    eventbus_publish(&ok);\n   239→    return 0;\n   240→}\n   241→```\n   242→\n   243→### 3.3. Example Steps – `src/steps_fx.c`, `src/steps_ledger.c`\n   244→\n   245→```c\n   246→/* src/steps_fx.c */\n   247→#include \"saga.h\"\n   248→#include <stdio.h>\n   249→\n   250→int fx_convert(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   251→{\n   252→    (void)payload; /* Not needed for demo */\n   253→    printf(\"💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   254→    /* simulate success */\n   255→    return 0;\n   256→}\n   257→\n   258→int fx_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   259→{\n   260→    (void)payload;\n   261→    printf(\"↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   262→    return 0;\n   263→}\n   264→```\n   265→\n   266→```c\n   267→/* src/steps_ledger.c */\n   268→#include \"saga.h\"\n   269→#include <stdio.h>\n   270→\n   271→int ledger_post(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   272→{\n   273→    (void)payload;\n   274→    printf(\"📒  Ledger posting OK for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   275→    return 0;\n   276→}\n   277→\n   278→int ledger_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   279→{\n   280→    (void)payload;\n   281→    printf(\"↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   282→    return 0;\n   283→}\n   284→```\n   285→\n   286→### 3.4. Demonstration Driver – `samples/payment_saga.c`\n   287→\n   288→```c\n   289→#include \"saga.h\"\n   290→#include <stdio.h>\n   291→#include <stdlib.h>\n   292→#include <time.h>\n   293→\n   294→/* ---------- Step Implementations --------------------------------------- */\n   295→extern int fx_convert(uint64_t, void **);\n   296→extern int fx_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n   297→\n   298→extern int ledger_post(uint64_t, void **);\n   299→extern int ledger_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n   300→\n   301→static int fraud_score(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   302→{\n   303→    (void)payload;\n   304→    printf(\"🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   305→\n   306→    /* Simulate 30% failure rate for classroom chaos monkeys */\n   307→    if (rand() % 10 < 3) {\n   308→        fprintf(stderr, \"🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\\n\");\n   309→        return -1;\n   310→    }\n   311→\n   312→    printf(\"✅  Fraud scoring passed\\n\");\n   313→    return 0;\n   314→}\n   315→\n   316→static int fraud_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   317→{\n   318→    (void)payload;\n   319→    printf(\"↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   320→    return 0;\n   321→}\n   322→\n   323→/* ---------- Audit Trail Console Subscriber ----------------------------- */\n   324→static void audit_logger(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx)\n   325→{\n   326→    (void)ctx;\n   327→    const char *type_str[] = {\n   328→        \"STEP_BEGIN\", \"STEP_DONE\", \"ABORT\", \"ROLLBACK_DONE\", \"SUCCESS\"\n   329→    };\n   330→    printf(\"📝  Audit: %s — saga=%lu step=%s\\n\",\n   331→           type_str[evt->type], evt->saga_id, evt->step_name);\n   332→}\n   333→\n   334→int main(void)\n   335→{\n   336→    srand((unsigned)time(NULL));\n   337→    eventbus_subscribe(audit_logger, NULL);\n   338→\n   339→    saga_step_def_t steps[] = {\n   340→        { \"FX_Conversion\",   fx_convert,   fx_reverse   },\n   341→        { \"Ledger_Posting\",  ledger_post,  ledger_reverse },\n   342→        { \"Fraud_Scoring\",   fraud_score,  fraud_reverse }\n   343→    };\n   344→\n   345→    payment_saga_t saga;\n   346→    saga_init(&saga, 42, steps, 3);\n   347→\n   348→    int rc = saga_execute(&saga);\n   349→    printf(rc == 0 ? \"🎉  Saga completed successfully!\\n\"\n   350→                   : \"💔  Saga rolled back.\\n\");\n   351→    return rc;\n   352→}\n   353→```\n   354→\n   355→Compile & Run:\n   356→\n   357→```bash\n   358→gcc -Iinclude src/saga.c src/steps_fx.c src/steps_ledger.c samples/payment_saga.c -lpthread -o bin/payment_saga\n   359→./bin/payment_saga\n   360→```\n   361→\n   362→Expected terminal output (with occasional induced failure):\n   363→\n   364→```\n   365→💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga 42\n   366→📒  Ledger posting OK for saga 42\n   367→🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga 42\n   368→🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\n   369→❌  Saga 42 failed at step Fraud_Scoring\n   370→↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga 42\n   371→↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga 42\n   372→↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga 42\n   373→💔  Saga rolled back.\n   374→```\n   375→\n   376→---\n   377→\n   378→## 4. Teaching Notes\n   379→\n   380→* The orchestrator resides **inside** the Payment bounded context, not in an external workflow engine.  \n   381→  Code can therefore be debugged with `gdb` by novices.\n   382→* Because the event bus is *in-process*, instructors can swap it for a\n   383→  real Kafka / NATS adapter during advanced labs without touching business logic.\n   384→* Compensations are **idempotent**: every reverse handler can run twice safely,\n   385→  a mandatory property in at-least-once delivery environments.\n   386→\n   387→---\n   388→\n   389→## 5. Production Hardening Checklist\n   390→\n   391→- [x] Persist `payment_saga_t` progress to EventStoreDB → replay after crash.  \n   392→- [x] Encrypt payloads (`payload_encrypt()`) for PCI-DSS compliance.  \n   393→- [x] Emit OpenTelemetry spans for distributed tracing.  \n   394→- [ ] Add circuit-breakers around downstream services (coming Q3).  \n   395→\n   396→---\n   397→\n   398→Happy hacking!  \n   399→EduPay Ledger Team ✨\n   400→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. 77→\n    78→/* --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    79→ * Registration\n    80→ * -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n    81→\n    82→/* Register a handler for a given command name.\n    83→ * A deep copy of `command_name` is stored internally, so the caller\n    84→ * may release it after the call returns.\n    85→ *\n    86→ * Returns:\n    87→ *  • EDU_CMD_SUCCESS  => registration successful\n    88→ *  • EDU_CMD_EEXISTS  => a handler for the command already exists\n    89→ *  • EDU_CMD_ENOMEM   => allocation failure\n    90→ *  • EDU_CMD_EINVAL   => invalid argument(s)\n    91→ */\n    92→int edu_command_bus_register(edu_command_bus_t     *bus,\n    93→                             const char            *command_name,\n    94→                             edu_command_handler_fn handler,\n    95→                             void                  *ctx);\n    96→\n    97→/* Unregister an existing handler for the command.\n    98→ * Returns EDU_CMD_SUCCESS if the handler was removed, or EDU_CMD_ENOHANDLER\n    99→ * if none was found.\n   100→ */\n   101→int edu_command_bus_unregister(edu_command_bus_t *bus,\n   102→                               const char        *command_name);\n   103→\n   104→/* --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   105→ * Dispatching\n   106→ * -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   107→\n   108→/* Dispatch a command.\n   109→ * Looks up the corresponding handler and invokes it with the provided\n   110→ * payload. Thread-safe; multiple concurrent dispatchers are allowed.\n   111→ *\n   112→ * Returns:\n   113→ *  • EDU_CMD_SUCCESS     => handler executed and returned success\n   114→ *  • EDU_CMD_ENOHANDLER  => no registered handler\n   115→ *  • EDU_CMD_EHANDLER    => handler ran but indicated failure\n   116→ */\n   117→int edu_command_bus_dispatch(edu_command_bus_t *bus,\n   118→                             const edu_command_t *cmd);\n   119→\n   120→/* --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   121→ * Utilities\n   122→ * -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   123→\n   124→/* Convert an edu_cmd_status_t to a human-readable, static error string. */\n   125→const char *edu_command_bus_strerror(int code);\n   126→\n   127→#ifdef __cplusplus\n   128→}\n   129→#endif\n   130→\n   131→/* --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   132→ * Implementation\n   133→ * -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   134→#ifdef EDUPAY_COMMAND_BUS_IMPLEMENTATION\n   135→#include <pthread.h>\n   136→#include <stdlib.h>\n   137→#include <string.h>\n   138→#include <errno.h>\n   139→\n   140→/* --- Internal helpers ---------------------------------------------------- */\n   141→static char *edu__strdup(const char *src)\n   142→{\n   143→#if defined(_POSIX_C_SOURCE) && _POSIX_C_SOURCE >= 200809L\n   144→    return src ? strdup(src) : NULL;\n   145→#else\n   146→    if (!src) return 154→/* Linked-list node mapping command_name → handler                       */\n   155→typedef struct edu_cmd_subscription {\n   156→    char                         *command_name;\n   157→    edu_command_handler_fn        handler;\n   158→    void                         *ctx;\n   159→    struct edu_cmd_subscription  *next;\n   160→} edu_cmd_subscription_t;\n   161→\n   162→struct edu_command_bus {\n   163→    edu_cmd_subscription_t *subs;  /* Head of linked list               */\n   164→    pthread_rwlock_t        lock;  /* Guards subs list                  */\n   165→};\n   166→\n   167→/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n   168→int edu_command_bus_create(edu_command_bus_t **out_bus)\n   169→{\n   170→    if (!out_bus) return (pthread_rwlock_init(&bus->lock, NULL) != 0) {\n   176→        free(bus);\n   177→        return EDU_CMD_ENOMEM;\n   178→    }\n   179→\n   180→    *out_bus = bus;\n   181→    return 186→{\n   187→    while (node) {\n   188→        edu_cmd_subscription_t *next = node->next;\n   189→        free(node->command_name);\n   190→        free(node);\n   191→        node = next;\n   192→    }\n   193→}\n   194→\n   195→void edu_command_bus_destroy(edu_command_bus_t *bus)\n   196→{\n   197→    if (!bus) return;\n   198→\n   199→    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&bus->lock);\n   200→ 206→}\n   207→\n   208→/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n   209→int edu_command_bus_register(edu_command_bus_t     *bus,\n   210→                             const char            *command_name,\n   211→                             edu_command_handler_fn handler,\n   212→                             void                  *ctx)\n   213→{\n   214→    if (edu_cmd_subscription_t *)calloc(1, sizeof(*node));\n   229→    if (!node) {\n   230→        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&bus->lock);\n   231→        return EDU_CMD_ENOMEM;\n   232→    }\n   233→\n   234→ bus->subs;\n   246→    bus->subs     = node;\n   247→\n   248→    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&bus->lock);\n   249→    return EDU_CMD_SUCCESS;\n   250→}\n   251→\n   252→/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n   253→int edu_command_bus_unregister(edu_command_bus_t *bus,\n   254→                               const char edu_command_bus_dispatch(edu_command_bus_t *bus,\n   282→                             const edu_command_t *cmd)\n   283→{\n   284→    if (!bus || !cmd || !cmd->name) return EDU_CMD_EINVAL;\n   285→\n void *ctx = NULL;\n   301→\n   302→    if (sub) {\n   303→        handler = sub->handler;\n   304→        ctx     = sub->ctx;\n   305→    }\n   306→\n   307→    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&bus->lock);\n   308→\n   309→    if (!handler) return EDU_CMD_ENOHANDLER;\n   310→\n   311→    int h_rc = handler(cmd, ctx);\n   312→    return (h_rc == EDU_CMD_SUCCESS) ? {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014J2eK5qz6AB5KAo1x1YmX8","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→/*\n     2→ * EduPay Ledger Academy\n     3→ * Shared Kernel / Domain / Money\n     4→ *\n     5→ * Copyright (c) 2023-2024 EduPay\n     6→ *\n     7→ * This file defines the canonical Money value-object used across every\n     8→ * bounded-context (Admissions, Bursar, Financial-Aid, etc.).  The structure is\n     9→ * purposely framework-agnostic so that educators can swap persistence layers,\n    10→ * message brokers, or UI shells without impacting business rules.\n    11→ *\n    12→ * Design notes\n    13→ * ------------\n    14→ * 1. Amount is stored in the *minor* unit (e.g., cents) as a 64-bit signed\n    15→ *    integer to avoid floating-point rounding errors.\n    16→ * 2. ISO-4217 alpha-3 currency codes are used.  Validation is built-in so\n    17→ *    erroneous codes are rejected early.\n    18→ * 3. All operations guarantee currency homogeneity; cross-currency math must\n    19→ *    be handled by the FX service in the multi-currency bounded context.\n    20→ * 4. Overflow checks leverage compiler intrinsics when available\n    21→ *    (__builtin_add_overflow / __builtin_mul_overflow).  Fallback logic is\n    22→ *    provided for non-GNU/Clang toolchains.\n    23→ * 5. This header is fully self-contained: implementation is declared `static\n    24→ *    inline` so that business-rule code can include it directly without\n    25→ *    linking.\n    26→ */\n    27→\n    28→#ifndef EDUPAY_LEDGER_ACADEMY_SHARED_KERNEL_DOMAIN_MONEY_H\n    29→#define EDUPAY_LEDGER_ACADEMY_SHARED_KERNEL_DOMAIN_MONEY_H\n    30→\n    31→/* NOLINTBEGIN (clang-tidy readability-identifier-naming) */\n    32→\n    33→#include <stdint.h>   /* int64_t, uint8_t, etc. */\n    34→#include <stdbool.h>  /* bool                    */\n    35→#include <stddef.h>   /* size_t                  */\n    36→#include <string.h>   /* memcpy, strlen          */\n    37→#include <stdio.h>    /* snprintf                */\n    38→#include <limits.h>   /* INT64_MAX               */\n    39→\n    40→#ifdef __cplusplus\n    41→extern \"C\" {\n    42→#endif\n    43→\n    44→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n    45→/* Public Types                                                               */\n    46→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n    47→\n    48→/* Maximum length of an ISO-4217 alpha-3 currency code including null byte. */\n    49→#define MONEY_CURRENCY_CODE_LEN 4\n    50→\n    51→/* Forward declaration for internal use. */\n    52→struct Money;\n    53→\n    54→/*\n    55→ * Result codes for all Money API calls.  Always check the returned status.\n    56→ * (Think of these as domain-level exceptions in languages that support them.)\n    57→ */\n    58→typedef enum\n    59→{\n    60→    MONEY_OK = 0,\n    61→    MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT,\n    62→    MONEY_ERR_INVALID_CURRENCY,\n    63→    MONEY_ERR_CURRENCY_MISMATCH,\n    64→    MONEY_ERR_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW\n    65→} MoneyError;\n    66→\n    67→/*\n    68→ * Canonical value-object.  Immutable by convention; all mutating operations\n    69→ * return a *new* instance so calling code can remain pure/functional.\n    70→ */\n    71→typedef struct Money\n    72→{\n    73→    int64_t amount_minor;                     /* e.g. cents, pence, satoshi   */\n    74→    char    currency[MONEY_CURRENCY_CODE_LEN];/* \"USD\", \"EUR\", \"JPY\", …      */\n    75→} Money;\n    76→\n    77→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n    78→/* Utility Macros                                                             */\n    79→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n    80→\n    81→/* Compiler-agnostic overflow helpers. */\n    82→#if defined(__GNUC__) || defined(__clang__)\n    83→    #define MONEY_ADD_OVERFLOW(a, b, result) __builtin_add_overflow((a), (b), (result))\n    84→    #define MONEY_MUL_OVERFLOW(a, b, result) __builtin_mul_overflow((a), (b), (result))\n    85→#else\n    86→    /* Portable—though slightly slower—fallbacks. */\n    87→    static inline bool MONEY_ADD_OVERFLOW(int64_t a, int64_t b, int64_t *r)\n    88→    {\n    89→        *r = a + b;\n    90→        return ((b > 0) && (a > INT64_MAX - b)) ||\n    91→               ((b < 0) && (a < INT64_MIN - b));\n    92→    }\n    93→\n    94→    static inline bool MONEY_MUL_OVERFLOW(int64_t a, int64_t b, int64_t *r)\n    95→    {\n    96→        /* Early outs for 0 and 1 to reduce branch cost. */\n    97→        if (a == 0 || b == 0) { *r = 0; return false; }\n    98→        if (a == 1)           { *r = b; return false; }\n    99→        if (b == 1)           { *r = a; return false; }\n   100→\n   101→        *r = a * b;\n   102→        return (a == (*r / b)) ? false : true;\n   103→    }\n   104→#endif\n   105→\n   106→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   107→/* Internal helpers (static inline, not exported)                             */\n   108→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   109→\n   110→/* Validates that `code` is an uppercase ISO-4217 alpha-3 currency code. */\n   111→static inline bool\n   112→_money_is_valid_currency(const char *code)\n   113→{\n   114→    if (!code) return false;\n   115→    size_t len = strlen(code);\n   116→    if (len != 3U) return false;\n   117→\n   118→    /* Ensure A-Z characters only. */\n   119→    for (size_t i = 0; i < 3; ++i)\n   120→    {\n   121→        char c = code[i];\n   122→        if (c < 'A' || c > 'Z') return false;\n   123→    }\n   124→    return true;\n   125→}\n   126→\n   127→/* Ensures two Money values share the same currency. */\n   128→static inline bool\n   129→_money_same_currency(const Money *a, const Money *b)\n   130→{\n   131→    return (a && b) && (strncmp(a->currency, b->currency, 3) == 0);\n   132→}\n   133→\n   134→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   135→/* API                                                                        */\n   136→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   137→\n   138→/*\n   139→ * money_create\n   140→ *\n   141→ * Construct a Money value from minor units (e.g., cents).\n   142→ * Example: money_create(&usd, \"USD\", 12345); // $123.45\n   143→ */\n   144→static inline MoneyError\n   145→money_create(Money *out, const char *currency, int64_t amount_minor)\n   146→{\n   147→    if (!out || !currency) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   148→    if (!_money_is_valid_currency(currency)) return MONEY_ERR_INVALID_CURRENCY;\n   149→\n   150→    memcpy(out->currency, currency, 3);\n   151→    out->currency[3] = '\\0';        /* Null-terminate */\n   152→    out->amount_minor = amount_minor;\n   153→    return MONEY_OK;\n   154→}\n   155→\n   156→/*\n   157→ * money_zero\n   158→ *\n   159→ * Creates a zero-value Money instance for the given currency.\n   160→ */\n   161→static inline MoneyError\n   162→money_zero(Money *out, const char *currency)\n   163→{\n   164→    return money_create(out, currency, 0);\n   165→}\n   166→\n   167→/*\n   168→ * money_add\n   169→ *\n   170→ * Adds two Money values with identical currency.  `out` may alias `a` or `b`.\n   171→ */\n   172→static inline MoneyError\n   173→money_add(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out)\n   174→{\n   175→    if (!a || !b || !out) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   176→    if (!_money_same_currency(a, b)) return MONEY_ERR_CURRENCY_MISMATCH;\n   177→\n   178→    int64_t result;\n   179→    if (MONEY_ADD_OVERFLOW(a->amount_minor, b->amount_minor, &result))\n   180→        return MONEY_ERR_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW;\n   181→\n   182→    memcpy(out, a, sizeof(Money));  /* Copy currency as well */\n   183→    out->amount_minor = result;\n   184→    return MONEY_OK;\n   185→}\n   186→\n   187→/*\n   188→ * money_subtract\n   189→ *\n   190→ * Subtracts `b` from `a`.  `out = a - b`\n   191→ */\n   192→static inline MoneyError\n   193→money_subtract(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out)\n   194→{\n   195→    if (!a || !b || !out) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   196→    if (!_money_same_currency(a, b)) return MONEY_ERR_CURRENCY_MISMATCH;\n   197→\n   198→    int64_t result;\n   199→    if (MONEY_ADD_OVERFLOW(a->amount_minor, -b->amount_minor, &result))\n   200→        return MONEY_ERR_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW;\n   201→\n   202→    memcpy(out, a, sizeof(Money));\n   203→    out->amount_minor = result;\n   204→    return MONEY_OK;\n   205→}\n   206→\n   207→/*\n   208→ * money_multiply\n   209→ *\n   210→ * Multiplies a Money amount by an integer factor.  Fractional scalars should\n   211→ * be handled by a dedicated rounding/FX service.\n   212→ */\n   213→static inline MoneyError\n   214→money_multiply(const Money *a, int64_t factor, Money *out)\n   215→{\n   216→    if (!a || !out) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   217→\n   218→    int64_t result;\n   219→    if (MONEY_MUL_OVERFLOW(a->amount_minor, factor, &result))\n   220→        return MONEY_ERR_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW;\n   221→\n   222→    memcpy(out, a, sizeof(Money));\n   223→    out->amount_minor = result;\n   224→    return MONEY_OK;\n   225→}\n   226→\n   227→/*\n   228→ * money_compare\n   229→ *\n   230→ * Lexicographically compares `a` and `b`:\n   231→ *   Returns <0 if a < b\n   232→ *           0 if a == b\n   233→ *           >0 if a > b\n   234→ * Currency mismatch results in INT32_MIN as a sentinel error (callers\n   235→ * should handle this explicitly).\n   236→ */\n   237→static inline int\n   238→money_compare(const Money *a, const Money *b)\n   239→{\n   240→    if (!a || !b) return INT32_MIN; /* Treat null as fatal mismatch */\n   241→    if (!_money_same_currency(a, b)) return INT32_MIN;\n   242→\n   243→    if (a->amount_minor < b->amount_minor) return -1;\n   244→    if (a->amount_minor > b->amount_minor) return 1;\n   245→    return 0;\n   246→}\n   247→\n   248→/*\n   249→ * money_is_zero\n   250→ *\n   251→ * Convenience helper.\n   252→ */\n   253→static inline bool\n   254→money_is_zero(const Money *value)\n   255→{\n   256→    return value && (value->amount_minor == 0);\n   257→}\n   258→\n   259→/*\n   260→ * money_to_string\n   261→ *\n   262→ * Renders a Money value as human-readable ASCII into `buf`:\n   263→ *   Example: \"USD 123.45\"\n   264→ *\n   265→ * The buffer must be at least 32 bytes to accommodate INT64_MIN and currency.\n   266→ * The function returns MONEY_OK on success or MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT.\n   267→ */\n   268→static inline MoneyError\n   269→money_to_string(const Money *value, char *buf, size_t buf_len)\n   270→{\n   271→    if (!value || !buf) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   272→    if (buf_len < 32)   return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT; /* caller guarantee */\n   273→\n   274→    /* Minor units to major (assumes 2-decimal currencies; adapt as needed). */\n   275→    int64_t abs_minor = value->amount_minor >= 0 ? value->amount_minor\n   276→                                                 : -value->amount_minor;\n   277→    int64_t major     = abs_minor / 100;\n   278→    int64_t minor     = abs_minor % 100;\n   279→\n   280→    int  written = snprintf(buf,\n   281→                            buf_len,\n   282→                            \"%s %s%\" PRId64 \".%02\" PRId64,\n   283→                            value->currency,\n   284→                            (value->amount_minor < 0) ? \"-\" : \"\",\n   285→                            major,\n   286→                            minor);\n   287→\n   288→    /* snprintf returns number of chars that *would* have been written. */\n   289→    return (written > 0 && (size_t)written < buf_len)\n   290→        ? MONEY_OK\n   291→        : MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT; /* buffer not large enough */\n   292→}\n   293→\n   294→/*\n   295→ * money_allocate\n   296→ *\n   297→ * Split `total` into `n` nearly-equal Money parts such that the sum of the\n   298→ * parts equals the original total and leftover pennies are distributed to the\n   299→ * first buckets.  This is useful when allocating bursary funds across\n   300→ * multi-recipient cohorts.\n   301→ *\n   302→ * Caller must provide an array `out_parts` of length `n`.\n   303→ */\n   304→static inline MoneyError\n   305→money_allocate(const Money *total,\n   306→               size_t        n,\n   307→               Money        *out_parts /* array[n] */)\n   308→{\n   309→    if (!total || !out_parts || n == 0) return MONEY_ERR_NULL_ARGUMENT;\n   310→\n   311→    int64_t quotient  = total->amount_minor / (int64_t)n;\n   312→    int64_t remainder = total->amount_minor % (int64_t)n;\n   313→\n   314→    MoneyError err;\n   315→    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i)\n   316→    {\n   317→        int64_t share = quotient + ((int64_t)i < remainder ? 1 : 0);\n   318→        err = money_create(&out_parts[i], total->currency, share);\n   319→        if (err != MONEY_OK) return err;\n   320→    }\n   321→    return MONEY_OK;\n   322→}\n   323→\n   324→#ifdef __cplusplus\n   325→} /* extern \"C\" */\n   326→#endif\n   327→\n   328→/* NOLINTEND (clang-tidy readability-identifier-naming) */\n   329→#endif /* EDUPAY_LEDGER_ACADEMY_SHARED_KERNEL_DOMAIN_MONEY_H */\n   330→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. (!g_saga_mode)\n   390→        return false;\n   391→\n   392→    if (++counter >= fail_after) {\n   393→        counter = 0;\n   394→        log_write(LOG_WARN,\n   395→                  \"Saga mode: true;\n   400→    }\n   401→    return false;\n   402→}\n   403→\n   404→/* Primary dispatcher */\n   405→static bool process_message(const Message *msg)\n   406→{\n   407→    ++g_metrics.events_processed;\n   408→\n   409→    if (strcmp(msg->type, \"TUITION_DUE\") == 0)\n   410→        return handle_tuition_due(msg->payload);\n   411→\n   412→    else if (strcmp(msg->type, \"PAYMENT_POSTED\") == 0)\n   413→        return handle_payment_posted(msg->payload);\n   414→\n   415→    else if (strcmp(msg->type, \"SHUTDOWN\") == 0) {\n   416→        log_write(LOG_INFO, return true; /* keep running */\n   423→    }\n   424→}\n   425→\n   426→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   427→/*                               Graceful Shutdown                            */\n   428→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   429→\n   430→static atomic_bool g_should_stop = ATOMIC_VAR_INIT(false);\n   431→\n   432→static void on_signal(int sig)\n   433→{\n   434→    (void)sig;\n   435→    atomic_store_explicit(&g_should_stop, true, memory_order_relaxed);\n   436→}\n   437→\n   438→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   439→/*                                    Main                                    */\n   440→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   441→\n   442→int main(int argc, char **argv)\n   443→{\n   444→    (void)argc; (void)argv;\n   445→\n   446→    /* Config ---------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   447→    BursarConfig cfg;\n   448→    cfg_set_defaults(&cfg);\n   449→    cfg_load_from_env(&cfg);\n   450→\n   451→    /* Logging ---------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   452→    log_init(cfg.log_level, stderr);\n   453→    log_write(LOG_INFO, \"%s v%s booting…\", SERVICE_NAME, SERVICE_VERSION);\n   454→\n   455→    /* Saga mode -------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   456→    g_saga_mode = (getenv(CFG_ENV_PREFIX \"SAGA_MODE\") != NULL);\n   457→\n   458→    /* Metrics ---------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   459→    metrics_init();\n   460→\n   461→    /* Signal handling sigaction(SIGINT,  &sa, NULL);\n   466→    sigaction(SIGTERM, &sa, NULL);\n   467→\n   468→    /* Event loop ------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   469→    Message msg;\n   470→    while (!atomic_load_explicit(&g_should_stop, memory_order_relaxed)) {\n   471→\n   472→        /* Non-blocking check if data ready on stdin */\n   473→        fd_set rfds;\n   474→        FD_ZERO(&rfds);\n   475→        FD_SET(STDIN_FILENO, &rfds);\n   476→        struct timeval tv = { .tv_sec = 0, .tv_usec = 250000 }; /* 250ms */\n   477→        int ret = select(STDIN_FILENO + 1,            break;\n   482→        }\n   483→\n   484→        if (ret == 0) continue; /* timeout – heartbeat */\n   485→\n   486→        if (!broker_receive(&msg)) {\n   487→            /* EOF reached
