Generates a RFC-4122 UUIDv4 string (NUL-terminated).\n    89→ * out must point to a buffer of at least EVENT_UUID_STRLEN bytes.\n    90→ */\n    91→static inline event_rc_t\n    92→event_uuid4(char out[EVENT_UUID_STRLEN])\n    93→{\n    94→    if (!out) return EVENT_ERR_INVALID_ARGUMENT;\n    95→\n    96→    const char *hex = \"0123456789abcdef\";\n    97→    uint8_t rnd[16];\n    98→\n    99→    /* Cheap non-cryptographic PRNG suitable for test rigs only.              */\n   100→    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof rnd; ++i)\n   101→        rnd[i] = (uint8_t)(rand() & 0xFF);\n   102→\n   103→    /* Set version(4) and variant(10) bits per RFC-4122                       */\n   104→    rnd[6] = (rnd[6] & 0x0F) | 0x40;\n   105→    rnd[8] = (rnd[8] & 0x3F) | 0x80;\n   106→\n   107→    size_t p = 0;\n   108→    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof rnd; ++i)\n   109→    {\n   110→        out[p++] = hex[rnd[i] >> 4];\n   111→        out[p++] = hex[rnd[i] & 0x0F];\n   112→\n   113→        if (p == 8 || The payload is copied into an owned\n   122→ * buffer; callers may free their original memory after this call returns.\n   123→ */\n   124→static inline event_t *\n   125→event_new(const char 130→          const char       *tenant_id,\n   131→          event_rc_t       *o_rc)\n   132→{\n   133→    if (!type || !aggregate_id ||\n   134→        payload_len > EVENT_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES)\n   135→    {\n   136→        if (o_rc) *o_rc = EVENT_ERR_INVALID_ARGUMENT;\n   137→        return NULL;\n   138→    }\n   139→\n   140→    event_t *ev = (event_t *)calloc(1, sizeof *ev);\n   141→    if       */\n   148→    event_uuid4(ev->meta.id);\n   149→    strncpy(ev->meta.aggregate_id, aggregate_id, EVENT_UUID_STRLEN - 1);\n   150→    strncpy(ev->meta.type, type, EVENT_TYPE_STRLEN - 1);\n CORE ARCHITECTURAL PATTERNS – CQRS, EVENT SOURCING, SAGA\n\n### 3.1 COMMAND QUERY RESPONSIBILITY SEGREGATION (CQRS)\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/cqrs/command_bus.h` (330 lines)\n\n**Architecture:**\n```\nCommand Path (Write Model)\n   ↓\nCommand Bus (dispatch) → Command Handler → Domain Aggregate\n   ↓\nEvent Store (append-only) → Event Published\n   ↓\nEvent Projectors (subscribed)\n   ↓\nRead Model (SQLite/Redis) ← Query Service (read path)\n```\n\n**Command Bus Features:**\n- Thread-safe dispatch using `pthread_rwlock_t`\n- Dynamic handler registration/deregistration\n- Linked-list based routing (O(n) lookup, acceptable for finite commands)\n- Error codes: SUCCESS, EINVAL, ENOMEM, EEXISTS, ENOHANDLER, EHANDLER\n\n**Command Definition (DTOs):**\n```c\ntypedef struct edu_command {\n    const char *name;         // Command key\n    const void *payload;      // Immutable payload\n    size_t      payload_sz;   // Payload size\n} edu_command_t;\n```\n\n**Query Bus:** `/app/project/src/core/cqrs/query_bus.h`\n- Similar architecture for read-side queries\n- Separate from command path for scalability\n\n### 3.2 EVENT SOURCING\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/event_sourcing/event_store_interface.h` (287 lines)\n\n**Core Concepts:**\n- **Immutable append-only event log** – all state changes recorded as events\n- **Aggregate versioning** – optimistic concurrency control via version numbers\n- **Snapshots** – periodic state snapshots to accelerate rehydration\n- **Live subscriptions** – at-least-once delivery to subscribers\n\n**Event Store Interface (V-Table Pattern):**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    event_store_result_t (*append_to_stream)(\n        event_store_t *store,\n        const char *stream_name,\n        const event_store_record_t *events,\n        size_t event_count,\n        uint64_t expected_version);\n    \n    event_store_result_t (*read_stream)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*read_all)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*set_snapshot)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*subscribe)(...);\n    // ... more operations\n} event_store_ops_t;\n```\n\n**Event Record Structure:**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    event_store_uuid_t event_id;\n    char aggregate_type[64];        // \"StudentAccount\", \"Ledger\", etc.\n    event_store_uuid_t aggregate_id;\n    uint64_t aggregate_version;     // Optimistic concurrency token\n    char event_type[64];            // \"StudentEnrolled\", \"PaymentAuthorized\", etc.\n    uint8_t *payload;               // JSON/MessagePack/Protobuf (opaque)\n    size_t payload_size;\n    time_t timestamp_utc;\n} event_store_record_t;\n```\n\n**Event Definition:** `/app/project/src/core/event_sourcing/event.h` (338 lines)\n- UUID v4 generation (RFC-4122 compliant)\n- Binary serialization with magic number (0xEDUPA7A)\n- Format: `[magic(4)][meta][payloadLen(4)][payload]`\n- Max payload: 4096 bytes (safety bound)\n\n**Idempotent Processing:**\n- Projection metadata tracks `last_sequence` to detect replayed events\n- At-least-once delivery from event bus guarantees no loss\n\n### 3.3 SAGA PATTERN IMPLEMENTATION\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/saga/saga_definition.h` (296 lines)\n\n**Saga Concept:**\nDistributed transaction across multiple services using compensating transactions (compensations triggered in reverse order on failure).\n\n**Saga Definition Structure:**\n```c\ntypedef struct saga_step {\n    uint8_t               id;\n    const char           *name;\n    saga_step_action_fn   forward_action;      // Primary business logic\n    saga_step_action_fn   compensation_action; // Rollback logic\n    saga_step_state_t     state;\n} saga_step_t;\n\ntypedef struct saga_definition {\n    const char    *saga_name;           // e.g., \"TuitionPayment\"\n    struct { uint16_t major, minor, patch; } version;\n    saga_step_t   steps[SAGA_MAX_STEPS];\n    uint8_t       step_count;\n} saga_definition_t;\n```\n\n**Saga Coordinator:** `/app/project/src/core/saga/saga_coordinator.h`\n- Orchestrates step execution\n- Manages state transitions\n- Triggers compensation chain on failure\n\n**Stipend Disbursement Saga Example:**\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/application/use_cases/disburse_stipend_saga.h`\n\n**Step Sequence:**\n1. **Validate eligibility** (no compensation needed)\n2. **Reserve budget** → store `reservation_id`\n   - Fail point 1: release budget\n3. **Initiate transfer** (payment network) → store `network_tx_id`\n   - Fail point 2: release budget, reverse transfer\n4. **Record ledger entry**\n   - Fail point 3: reverse transfer, release budget, record reversal\n5. **Publish domain event**\n   - Fail point 4: all compensations (record reversal, reverse transfer, release budget)\n\n**Compensation Characteristics:**\n- **Idempotent** – safe to run multiple times\n- **Reverse order** – step 4 compensation runs before step 3's, etc.\n- **Error propagation** – maintains error context through chain\n\n**VTable Injection Pattern:**\n```c\ntypedef bool (*validate_eligibility_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*reserve_budget_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*release_budget_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*initiate_transfer_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*reverse_transfer_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*record_ledger_entry_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*publish_domain_event_fn)(...);\n\ntypedef struct {\n    validate_eligibility_fn validate_eligibility;\n    reserve_budget_fn reserve_budget;\n    release_budget_fn release_budget;\n    initiate_transfer_fn initiate_transfer;\n    reverse_transfer_fn reverse_transfer;\n    record_ledger_entry_fn record_ledger_entry;\n    publish_domain_event_fn publish_domain_event;\n} stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t;\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. SHARED KERNEL – VALUE OBJECTS & UTILITIES\n\n**Path:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/`\n\n### 4.1 Domain Value Objects\n\n**Money (Value Object):** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/money.h` (330 lines)\n\n**Purpose:** Cross-context canonical representation of monetary amounts\n\n**Features:**\n- **Immutable by convention** – all operations return new instances\n- **Minor units storage** – amounts in cents/pence to avoid floating-point errors\n- **ISO-4217 currency codes** – 3-letter codes (USD, EUR, JPY, etc.)\n- **Overflow detection** – uses `__builtin_add_overflow` / `__builtin_mul_overflow` (GCC/Clang) with fallback\n- **Header-only** – no external dependencies (C standard library only)\n\n**API:**\n```c\nMoneyError money_create(Money *out, const char *currency, int64_t amount_minor);\nMoneyError money_add(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out);\nMoneyError money_subtract(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out);\nMoneyError money_multiply(const Money *a, int64_t factor, Money *out);\nint money_compare(const Money *a, const Money *b);\nMoneyError money_allocate(const Money *total, size_t n, Money *out_parts);\n```\n\n**Example: Allocating bursary across N recipients**\n```c\nMoney total = { .amount_minor = 100000, .currency = \"USD\" };\nMoney parts[5];\nmoney_allocate(&total, 5, parts);  // Distributes with remainder handling\n```\n\n**Other Value Objects:**\n- `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/student_id.h` – Canonical student identifier\n- `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/transaction_id.h` – Transaction reference\n\n### 4.2 Infrastructure Utilities\n\n**Cryptography:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/infrastructure/crypto/`\n- `aescrypto.h` – AES-256-GCM for PII encryption (FERPA compliance)\n- `hash.h` – Secure hashing\n\n**Logging:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/infrastructure/logger.h`\n- Structured logging with log levels\n- Thread-safe output\n\n---\n\n## 5. KEY ARCHITECTURAL PATTERNS\n\n### 5.1 Clean Architecture Layering\n\n**Dependency Rule:** Outer layers know inner layers, never vice-versa.\n\n**Layers (from innermost to outermost):**\n\n1. **Domain Layer** (most stable, no I/O)\n   - Value objects (Money, StudentId)\n   - Business rules (payment logic, eligibility checks)\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/domain/`\n\n2. **Application Layer** (use cases)\n   - Orchestrates domain objects\n   - Translates DTOs to domain objects\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/application/use_cases/process_tuition_payment.h`\n   - Use case entry point with injected ports (fraud check, payment gateway, ledger)\n\n3. **Interface Layer** (replaceable)\n   - HTTP controllers, gRPC adapters, CLI handlers\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/interfaces/controllers/http_payment_controller.h`\n   - JSON presenters for API responses\n\n4. **Infrastructure Layer** (volatile, frameworks)\n   - PostgreSQL event store, RabbitMQ publishers, payment gateways\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/infrastructure/persistence/postgres_event_store.h`\n\n### 5.2 Dependency Inversion via Function Pointers\n\n**Pattern:** All I/O dependencies expressed as `struct` containing function pointers (V-tables).\n\n**Example from bursar service:**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    port_fraud_check_t       fraud_check;\n    port_reserve_funds_t     reserve_funds;\n    port_post_ledger_entry_t post_ledger;\n    port_write_audit_trail_t write_audit;\n} process_tuition_payment_interactor_t;\n\n// Called as:\nstatic inline edu_tuition_status_t process_tuition_payment(\n    const process_tuition_payment_interactor_t *interactor,\n    const process_tuition_payment_request_t *request,\n    process_tuition_payment_response_t *response) {\n    // ... business logic ...\n    if (!interactor->fraud_check(request, fraud_reason)) { ... }\n    if (!interactor->reserve_funds(request, txn_id, gateway_error)) { ... }\n}\n```\n\n**Benefits:**\n- **Testability** – inject mocks without recompilation\n- **Swappability** – replace PostgreSQL with SQLite for labs\n- **Framework independence** – core domain touches zero frameworks\n\n### 5.3 Message Bus & Event Publishing\n\n**Broker Stub (Pedagogical):** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/main.c` lines 177-224\n\nFor teaching purposes, the broker is modeled as STDIN/STDOUT:\n- Events received on STDIN (line-delimited format)\n- Events published to STDOUT\n- Professors swap in real AMQP/Kafka without touching business logic\n\n**Example Protocol:**\n```\nInput:  TUITION_DUE student_id=S123 amount=12500 currency=USD\nOutput: LEDGER_UPDATED student_id=S123 balance=12500 currency=USD\n```\n\n### 5.4 Idempotency & At-Least-Once Delivery\n\n**Pattern:** Event projectors track processed sequences\n\n**Dashboard Projector Implementation:**\n```c\n// In projection_metadata table:\nCREATE TABLE projection_metadata (\n    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,\n    last_sequence INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0\n);\n\n// On event arrival:\nif (evt->sequence <= last_seq) return; // Already processed\n// ... apply fold ...\n_update_last_sequence(db, evt->sequence);\n```\n\n**Guarantees:**\n- If an event is delivered twice, it's folded only once\n- On projector restart, replay from last known position\n- Prevents double-counting in read models\n\n---\n\n## 6. DATA FLOW – COMPLETE EXAMPLE\n\n### Scenario: Student Pays Tuition\n\n**Happy Path:**\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐\n│ Student Portal  │ POST /tuition/pay\n└────────┬────────┘\n         │ {student_id, amount, currency, payment_method}\n         ↓\n    ┌─────────────────────┐\n    │ Bursar API Gateway  │\n    │ (HTTP Controller)   │\n    └────────┬────────────┘\n             │ Extract request DTO\n             ↓\n    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n    │ use_case: process_tuition_payment()      │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │1. Fraud Check (injected port)       │ │\n    │ │   if (!fraud_check(...)) { ERROR }  │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │2. Reserve Funds (gateway port)       │ │\n    │ │   gateway->authorize_card(...)       │ │\n    │ │   → txn_id = \"ABC123\"                │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │3. Post Ledger Entry (domain logic)   │ │\n    │ │   invoice_id → Ledger entry          │ │\n    │ │   → event: PaymentAuthorized         │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │4. Write Audit Trail (infrastructure)│ │\n    │ │   → event_store.append(...)          │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    └──────────────┬───────────────────────────┘\n                   │ Response: TUITION_OK, txn_id\n                   ↓\n    ┌──────────────────────────┐\n    │ Publish Event to Bus     │\n    │ event: PaymentAuthorized │\n    └────────┬─────────────────┘\n             │\n             ├─────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐\n             ↓                     ↓                      ↓\n    ┌──────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐\n    │  Event Store │     │ Projectors      │   │ Subscribers │\n    │  (append)    │     │ (fold events)   │   │  (services) │\n    └──────────────┘     └─────────────────┘   └─────────────┘\n         (WRITE)              (CQRS)           (integrations)\n             │                    │\n             ↓                    ↓\n    ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────────┐\n    │ Audit Trail  │     │ Dashboard Read   │\n    │ (immutable)  │     │ Model (SQLite)   │\n    └──────────────┘     └──────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Key Observations:**\n1. **Fraud check first** – reject early before gateway calls\n2. **Gateway interaction outside ledger** – network failures don't affect core transaction\n3. **Ledger entry post-authorization** – only committed amounts recorded\n4. **Audit trail separate** – audit failures don't roll back payments\n5. **Event published** – async projections update read models\n\n---\n\n## 7. ARCHITECTURAL HIGHLIGHTS\n\n### 9.1 Why C?\n- **Direct control** – memory management teaches resource discipline\n- **No magic** – students see exact I/O, concurrency, serialization\n- **Educational** – runtime semantics visible (no JIT, GC, reflection surprises)\n- **Deployment** – tiny binaries, low latency (suitable for fintech)\n\n### 9.2 Framework Independence\n- **Zero external dependencies in domain** – only C17 standard library\n- **Adapters at periphery** – PostgreSQL, RabbitMQ, HTTP swapped without touching rules\n- **Testing friendly** – mock ports without framework setup\n\n### 9.3 Compliance Baked In\n- **PCI-DSS** – tokenization gateway, PAN never in core process memory\n- **FERPA** – AES-256-GCM encryption for PII, key rotation via Vault\n- **PSD2** – SCA patterns, WebAuthn support for continuing-education\n\n### 9.4 Saga Demo Mode\n**Feature:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/main.c` line 456\n\nEnable with: `export EDUPAY_SAGA_MODE=1`\n\nSimulates failures every N events, forcing compensation chain execution. Professors use this to teach rollback scenarios in live labs.\n\n---\n\n## 10. HOW SERVICES COMMUNICATE\n\n### Event-Driven (Asynchronous)\n\n**Flow:**\n1. **Bursar publishes** `PaymentAuthorized` to message bus (NATS/Kafka/AMQP)\n2. **Financial Aid** subscribed to this event, triggers `disburse_stipend_saga`\n3. **Projections Service** subscribes to all events, folds into SQLite dashboard\n\n### Command-Query Separation\n\n**Write Path:**\n- HTTP POST /tuition/pay → Command Bus → Use Case → Domain Logic → Event Store\n\n**Read Path:**\n- HTTP GET /dashboard → Query Bus → Read Model (SQLite) → JSON Presenter\n\n**Benefits:**\n- Different persistence for writes (event log) vs. reads (denormalized index)\n- Projections lag writes but scale reads independently\n- Eventually consistent dashboards acceptable for reporting\n\n---\n\n## 11. DEPLOYMENT & EXTENSIBILITY\n\n### Swappable Infrastructure Examples\n\n**1. Replace Event Store:**\n```c\n// Today: PostgreSQL\nstruct event_store *store = postgres_event_store_create(...);\n\n// Tomorrow: SQLite (for lab exercises)\nstruct event_store *store = sqlite_event_store_create(...);\n\n// Code using store unchanged:\nevent_store_append(store, stream, events, count, version);\n```\n\n**2. Replace Message Broker:**\n```c\n// Today: STDIN/STDOUT (pedagogical)\nvoid broker_publish(const Message *msg) { fprintf(stdout, ...); }\n\n// Tomorrow: RabbitMQ\nvoid broker_publish(const Message *msg) { amqp_basic_publish(...); }\n\n// Bursar service unchanged:\nif (!process_message(&msg)) break;\n```\n\n**3. Replace Crypto:**\n- Default: AES-256-GCM (OpenSSL)\n- Lab: XOR cipher (insecure, for teaching only)\n- Production: Hardware security module (HSM) via PKCS#11\n\n---\n\n## 12. buffer)\n- SQLite instead of PostgreSQL\n\n### End-to-End Tests\n- Docker Compose (full stack)\n- Real PostgreSQL + RabbitMQ\n- Chaos injection (kill services mid-saga)\n\n---\n\n## FILE PATH `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/application/commands/process_payment_command.h`\n  - `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/application/queries/get_account_balance_query.h`\n\n- **Infrastructure:**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/infrastructure/persistence/postgres_event_store.h`\n  - `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/infrastructure/gateways/pci_dss_proxy.h` - Tokenizes payment data\n\n- **Interfaces:**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/interfaces/controllers/http_payment_controller.h`\n  - `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/interfaces/presenters/json_presenters.h`\n\n### 2.2 FINANCIAL AID SERVICE\n**Path:** `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/`\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Scholarship award calculations\n- Stipend disbursement orchestration\n- KYC/AML compliance verification\n- Fraud detection and risk scoring\n- Integration with payment networks\n\n**Key Files:**\n- **Main Entry Point:** `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/main.c`\n\n- **Domain Entities:**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/domain/scholarship_fund.h`\n  - `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/domain/stipend.h`\n\n- **Saga Implementation (Critical):**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/application/use_cases/disburse_stipend_saga.h` (362 lines)\n    - **5-step saga orchestration:**\n      1. Validate eligibility\n      2. Reserve budget (allocation ledger)\n      3. Initiate transfer (payment network)\n      4. Record ledger entry (book-keeping)\n      5. Publish domain event (CQRS)\n    - **Compensation paths:** Budget release, transfer reversal, ledger reversal\n    - Header-only with no external dependencies beyond C standard library\n    - VTable-based callback injection for all I/O operations\n\n- **Use Cases:**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/application/use_cases/verify_kyc.h`\n\n- **Infrastructure:**\n  - `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/infrastructure/messaging/rabbitmq_publisher.h`\n\n### 2.3 PROJECTIONS SERVICE\n**Path:** `/app/project/src/projections_service/`\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Read-model materialization (CQRS)\n- Event-driven dashboard updates\n- Real-time analytics and risk assessment\n\n**Key Event Handlers:**\n- `/app/project/src/projections_service/event_handlers/dashboard_projector.h` (430 lines)\n  - Highly-denormalized SQLite schema for query performance\n  - Idempotent replay guard (at-least-once delivery)\n  - Thread-safe fold operations with mutex synchronization\n  - Event types: PAYMENT_PROCESSED, TRANSACTION_SETTLED, FRAUD_FLAGGED, SCHOLARSHIP_DISBURSED\n\n- `/app/project/src/projections_service/event_handlers/risk_assessment_projector.c`\n\n---\n\n## 3. CORE ARCHITECTURAL PATTERNS – CQRS, EVENT SOURCING, SAGA\n\n### 3.1 COMMAND QUERY RESPONSIBILITY SEGREGATION (CQRS)\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/cqrs/command_bus.h` (330 lines)\n\n**Architecture:**\n```\nCommand Path (Write Model)\n   ↓\nCommand Bus (dispatch) → Command Handler → Domain Aggregate\n   ↓\nEvent Store (append-only) → Event Published\n   ↓\nEvent Projectors (subscribed)\n   ↓\nRead Model (SQLite/Redis) ← Query Service (read path)\n```\n\n**Command Bus Features:**\n- Thread-safe dispatch using `pthread_rwlock_t`\n- Dynamic handler registration/deregistration\n- Linked-list based routing (O(n) lookup, acceptable for finite commands)\n- Error codes: SUCCESS, EINVAL, ENOMEM, EEXISTS, ENOHANDLER, EHANDLER\n\n**Command Definition (DTOs):**\n```c\ntypedef struct edu_command {\n    const char *name;         // Command key\n    const void *payload;      // Immutable payload\n    size_t      payload_sz;   // Payload size\n} edu_command_t;\n```\n\n**Query Bus:** `/app/project/src/core/cqrs/query_bus.h`\n- Similar architecture for read-side queries\n- Separate from command path for scalability\n\n### 3.2 EVENT SOURCING\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/event_sourcing/event_store_interface.h` (287 lines)\n\n**Core Concepts:**\n- **Immutable append-only event log** – all state changes recorded as events\n- **Aggregate versioning** – optimistic concurrency control via version numbers\n- **Snapshots** – periodic state snapshots to accelerate rehydration\n- **Live subscriptions** – at-least-once delivery to subscribers\n\n**Event Store Interface (V-Table Pattern):**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    event_store_result_t (*append_to_stream)(\n        event_store_t *store,\n        const char *stream_name,\n        const event_store_record_t *events,\n        size_t event_count,\n        uint64_t expected_version);\n    \n    event_store_result_t (*read_stream)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*read_all)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*set_snapshot)(...);\n    event_store_result_t (*subscribe)(...);\n    // ... more operations\n} event_store_ops_t;\n```\n\n**Event Record Structure:**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    event_store_uuid_t event_id;\n    char aggregate_type[64];        // \"StudentAccount\", \"Ledger\", etc.\n    event_store_uuid_t aggregate_id;\n    uint64_t aggregate_version;     // Optimistic concurrency token\n    char event_type[64];            // \"StudentEnrolled\", \"PaymentAuthorized\", etc.\n    uint8_t *payload;               // JSON/MessagePack/Protobuf (opaque)\n    size_t payload_size;\n    time_t timestamp_utc;\n} event_store_record_t;\n```\n\n**Event Definition:** `/app/project/src/core/event_sourcing/event.h` (338 lines)\n- UUID v4 generation (RFC-4122 compliant)\n- Binary serialization with magic number (0xEDUPA7A)\n- Format: `[magic(4)][meta][payloadLen(4)][payload]`\n- Max payload: 4096 bytes (safety bound)\n\n**Idempotent Processing:**\n- Projection metadata tracks `last_sequence` to detect replayed events\n- At-least-once delivery from event bus guarantees no loss\n\n### 3.3 SAGA PATTERN IMPLEMENTATION\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/core/saga/saga_definition.h` (296 lines)\n\n**Saga Concept:**\nDistributed transaction across multiple services using compensating transactions (compensations triggered in reverse order on failure).\n\n**Saga Definition Structure:**\n```c\ntypedef struct saga_step {\n    uint8_t               id;\n    const char           *name;\n    saga_step_action_fn   forward_action;      // Primary business logic\n    saga_step_action_fn   compensation_action; // Rollback logic\n    saga_step_state_t     state;\n} saga_step_t;\n\ntypedef struct saga_definition {\n    const char    *saga_name;           // e.g., \"TuitionPayment\"\n    struct { uint16_t major, minor, patch; } version;\n    saga_step_t   steps[SAGA_MAX_STEPS];\n    uint8_t       step_count;\n} saga_definition_t;\n```\n\n**Saga Coordinator:** `/app/project/src/core/saga/saga_coordinator.h`\n- Orchestrates step execution\n- Manages state transitions\n- Triggers compensation chain on failure\n\n**Stipend Disbursement Saga Example:**\n\n**File:** `/app/project/src/services/financial_aid_service/application/use_cases/disburse_stipend_saga.h`\n\n**Step Sequence:**\n1. **Validate eligibility** (no compensation needed)\n2. **Reserve budget** → store `reservation_id`\n   - Fail point 1: release budget\n3. **Initiate transfer** (payment network) → store `network_tx_id`\n   - Fail point 2: release budget, reverse transfer\n4. **Record ledger entry**\n   - Fail point 3: reverse transfer, release budget, record reversal\n5. **Publish domain event**\n   - Fail point 4: all compensations (record reversal, reverse transfer, release budget)\n\n**Compensation Characteristics:**\n- **Idempotent** – safe to run multiple times\n- **Reverse order** – step 4 compensation runs before step 3's, etc.\n- **Error propagation** – maintains error context through chain\n\n**VTable Injection Pattern:**\n```c\ntypedef bool (*validate_eligibility_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*reserve_budget_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*release_budget_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*initiate_transfer_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*reverse_transfer_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*record_ledger_entry_fn)(...);\ntypedef bool (*publish_domain_event_fn)(...);\n\ntypedef struct {\n    validate_eligibility_fn validate_eligibility;\n    reserve_budget_fn reserve_budget;\n    release_budget_fn release_budget;\n    initiate_transfer_fn initiate_transfer;\n    reverse_transfer_fn reverse_transfer;\n    record_ledger_entry_fn record_ledger_entry;\n    publish_domain_event_fn publish_domain_event;\n} stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t;\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. SHARED KERNEL – VALUE OBJECTS & UTILITIES\n\n**Path:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/`\n\n### 4.1 Domain Value Objects\n\n**Money (Value Object):** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/money.h` (330 lines)\n\n**Purpose:** Cross-context canonical representation of monetary amounts\n\n**Features:**\n- **Immutable by convention** – all operations return new instances\n- **Minor units storage** – amounts in cents/pence to avoid floating-point errors\n- **ISO-4217 currency codes** – 3-letter codes (USD, EUR, JPY, etc.)\n- **Overflow detection** – uses `__builtin_add_overflow` / `__builtin_mul_overflow` (GCC/Clang) with fallback\n- **Header-only** – no external dependencies (C standard library only)\n\n**API:**\n```c\nMoneyError money_create(Money *out, const char *currency, int64_t amount_minor);\nMoneyError money_add(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out);\nMoneyError money_subtract(const Money *a, const Money *b, Money *out);\nMoneyError money_multiply(const Money *a, int64_t factor, Money *out);\nint money_compare(const Money *a, const Money *b);\nMoneyError money_allocate(const Money *total, size_t n, Money *out_parts);\n```\n\n**Example: Allocating bursary across N recipients**\n```c\nMoney total = { .amount_minor = 100000, .currency = \"USD\" };\nMoney parts[5];\nmoney_allocate(&total, 5, parts);  // Distributes with remainder handling\n```\n\n**Other Value Objects:**\n- `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/student_id.h` – Canonical student identifier\n- `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/domain/transaction_id.h` – Transaction reference\n\n### 4.2 Infrastructure Utilities\n\n**Cryptography:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/infrastructure/crypto/`\n- `aescrypto.h` – AES-256-GCM for PII encryption (FERPA compliance)\n- `hash.h` – Secure hashing\n\n**Logging:** `/app/project/src/shared_kernel/infrastructure/logger.h`\n- Structured logging with log levels\n- Thread-safe output\n\n---\n\n## 5. KEY ARCHITECTURAL PATTERNS\n\n### 5.1 Clean Architecture Layering\n\n**Dependency Rule:** Outer layers know inner layers, never vice-versa.\n\n**Layers (from innermost to outermost):**\n\n1. **Domain Layer** (most stable, no I/O)\n   - Value objects (Money, StudentId)\n   - Business rules (payment logic, eligibility checks)\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/domain/`\n\n2. **Application Layer** (use cases)\n   - Orchestrates domain objects\n   - Translates DTOs to domain objects\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/application/use_cases/process_tuition_payment.h`\n   - Use case entry point with injected ports (fraud check, payment gateway, ledger)\n\n3. **Interface Layer** (replaceable)\n   - HTTP controllers, gRPC adapters, CLI handlers\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/interfaces/controllers/http_payment_controller.h`\n   - JSON presenters for API responses\n\n4. **Infrastructure Layer** (volatile, frameworks)\n   - PostgreSQL event store, RabbitMQ publishers, payment gateways\n   - **Example:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/infrastructure/persistence/postgres_event_store.h`\n\n### 5.2 Dependency Inversion via Function Pointers\n\n**Pattern:** All I/O dependencies expressed as `struct` containing function pointers (V-tables).\n\n**Example from bursar service:**\n```c\ntypedef struct {\n    port_fraud_check_t       fraud_check;\n    port_reserve_funds_t     reserve_funds;\n    port_post_ledger_entry_t post_ledger;\n    port_write_audit_trail_t write_audit;\n} process_tuition_payment_interactor_t;\n\n// Called as:\nstatic inline edu_tuition_status_t process_tuition_payment(\n    const process_tuition_payment_interactor_t *interactor,\n    const process_tuition_payment_request_t *request,\n    process_tuition_payment_response_t *response) {\n    // ... business logic ...\n    if (!interactor->fraud_check(request, fraud_reason)) { ... }\n    if (!interactor->reserve_funds(request, txn_id, gateway_error)) { ... }\n}\n```\n\n**Benefits:**\n- **Testability** – inject mocks without recompilation\n- **Swappability** – replace PostgreSQL with SQLite for labs\n- **Framework independence** – core domain touches zero frameworks\n\n### 5.3 Message Bus & Event Publishing\n\n**Broker Stub (Pedagogical):** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/main.c` lines 177-224\n\nFor teaching purposes, the broker is modeled as STDIN/STDOUT:\n- Events received on STDIN (line-delimited format)\n- Events published to STDOUT\n- Professors swap in real AMQP/Kafka without touching business logic\n\n**Example Protocol:**\n```\nInput:  TUITION_DUE student_id=S123 amount=12500 currency=USD\nOutput: LEDGER_UPDATED student_id=S123 balance=12500 currency=USD\n```\n\n### 5.4 Idempotency & At-Least-Once Delivery\n\n**Pattern:** Event projectors track processed sequences\n\n**Dashboard Projector Implementation:**\n```c\n// In projection_metadata table:\nCREATE TABLE projection_metadata (\n    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,\n    last_sequence INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0\n);\n\n// On event arrival:\nif (evt->sequence <= last_seq) return; // Already processed\n// ... apply fold ...\n_update_last_sequence(db, evt->sequence);\n```\n\n**Guarantees:**\n- If an event is delivered twice, it's folded only once\n- On projector restart, replay from last known position\n- Prevents double-counting in read models\n\n---\n\n## 6. DATA FLOW – COMPLETE EXAMPLE\n\n### Scenario: Student Pays Tuition\n\n**Happy Path:**\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐\n│ Student Portal  │ POST /tuition/pay\n└────────┬────────┘\n         │ {student_id, amount, currency, payment_method}\n         ↓\n    ┌─────────────────────┐\n    │ Bursar API Gateway  │\n    │ (HTTP Controller)   │\n    └────────┬────────────┘\n             │ Extract request DTO\n             ↓\n    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n    │ use_case: process_tuition_payment()      │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │1. Fraud Check (injected port)       │ │\n    │ │   if (!fraud_check(...)) { ERROR }  │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │2. Reserve Funds (gateway port)       │ │\n    │ │   gateway->authorize_card(...)       │ │\n    │ │   → txn_id = \"ABC123\"                │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │3. Post Ledger Entry (domain logic)   │ │\n    │ │   invoice_id → Ledger entry          │ │\n    │ │   → event: PaymentAuthorized         │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    │ ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n    │ │4. Write Audit Trail (infrastructure)│ │\n    │ │   → event_store.append(...)          │ │\n    │ └──────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n    └──────────────┬───────────────────────────┘\n                   │ Response: TUITION_OK, txn_id\n                   ↓\n    ┌──────────────────────────┐\n    │ Publish Event to Bus     │\n    │ event: PaymentAuthorized │\n    └────────┬─────────────────┘\n             │\n             ├─────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐\n             ↓                     ↓                      ↓\n    ┌──────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐\n    │  Event Store │     │ Projectors      │   │ Subscribers │\n    │  (append)    │     │ (fold events)   │   │  (services) │\n    └──────────────┘     └─────────────────┘   └─────────────┘\n         (WRITE)              (CQRS)           (integrations)\n             │                    │\n             ↓                    ↓\n    ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────────┐\n    │ Audit Trail  │     │ Dashboard Read   │\n    │ (immutable)  │     │ Model (SQLite)   │\n    └──────────────┘     └──────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Key Observations:**\n1. **Fraud check first** – reject early before gateway calls\n2. **Gateway interaction outside ledger** – network failures don't affect core transaction\n3. **Ledger entry post-authorization** – only committed amounts recorded\n4. **Audit trail separate** – audit failures don't roll back payments\n5. **Event published** – async projections update read models\n\n---\n\n## 7. DOCUMENT STRUCTURE – ARCHITECTURE GUIDES\n\n**Path:** `/app/project/docs/architecture/`\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `01_clean_architecture.md` | Layering & dependency rules |\n| `02_microservices_and_bounded_contexts.md` | Service boundaries & data ownership |\n| `03_cqrs_and_event_sourcing.md` | Write/read model separation, event store |\n| `04_saga_pattern.md` | Distributed transaction coordination |\n| `05_security_and_compliance.md` | PCI-DSS, FERPA, PSD2 patterns |\n\n**Educational Modules:** `/app/project/docs/lessons/`\n\n| File | Topic |\n|------|-------|\n| `L01_Entities_and_Value_Objects.md` | DDD fundamentals |\n| `L02_Payment_Processing_Flow.md` | Use case orchestration |\n| `L03_Regulatory_Compliance_in_Code.md` | Real-world constraints |\n| `L04_Fraud_Detection_Heuristics.md` | Anomaly detection |\n| `L05_Debugging_Distributed_Sagas.md` | Tracing & testing failures |\n| `L06_Building_CQRS_Projections.md` | Read model construction |\n\n---\n\n## 8. KEY COMPONENT SUMMARY TABLE\n\n| Component | Location | Lines | Purpose |\n|-----------|----------|-------|---------|\n| **Command Bus** | `src/core/cqrs/command_bus.h` | 330 | Thread-safe command dispatch w/ dynamic registration |\n| **Event Store Interface** | `src/core/event_sourcing/event_store_interface.h` | 287 | Pluggable event persistence (Postgres, DynamoDB, etc.) |\n| **Event Definition** | `src/core/event_sourcing/event.h` | 338 | UUID v4 generation, serialization, binary format |\n| **Saga Definition** | `src/core/saga/saga_definition.h` | 296 | Step composition, state machine, compensation tracking |\n| **Money Value Object** | `src/shared_kernel/domain/money.h` | 330 | Immutable monetary amounts, overflow detection |\n| **Bursar Main** | `src/services/bursar_service/main.c` | 502 | Service entry point, event loop, configuration |\n| **Process Tuition UC** | `src/services/bursar_service/application/use_cases/process_tuition_payment.h` | 339 | Payment orchestration with dependency injection |\n| **Stipend Saga** | `src/services/financial_aid_service/application/use_cases/disburse_stipend_saga.h` | 362 | 5-step saga with compensation chain |\n| **Dashboard Projector** | `src/projections_service/event_handlers/dashboard_projector.h` | 430 | SQLite read model folding, idempotent replay |\n\n---\n\n## 9. ARCHITECTURAL HIGHLIGHTS\n\n### 9.1 Why C?\n- **Direct control** – memory management teaches resource discipline\n- **No magic** – students see exact I/O, concurrency, serialization\n- **Educational** – runtime semantics visible (no JIT, GC, reflection surprises)\n- **Deployment** – tiny binaries, low latency (suitable for fintech)\n\n### 9.2 Framework Independence\n- **Zero external dependencies in domain** – only C17 standard library\n- **Adapters at periphery** – PostgreSQL, RabbitMQ, HTTP swapped without touching rules\n- **Testing friendly** – mock ports without framework setup\n\n### 9.3 Compliance Baked In\n- **PCI-DSS** – tokenization gateway, PAN never in core process memory\n- **FERPA** – AES-256-GCM encryption for PII, key rotation via Vault\n- **PSD2** – SCA patterns, WebAuthn support for continuing-education\n\n### 9.4 Saga Demo Mode\n**Feature:** `/app/project/src/services/bursar_service/main.c` line 456\n\nEnable with: `export EDUPAY_SAGA_MODE=1`\n\nSimulates failures every N events, forcing compensation chain execution. Professors use this to teach rollback scenarios in live labs.\n\n---\n\n## 10. HOW SERVICES COMMUNICATE\n\n### Event-Driven (Asynchronous)\n\n**Flow:**\n1. **Bursar publishes** `PaymentAuthorized` to message bus (NATS/Kafka/AMQP)\n2. **Financial Aid** subscribed to this event, triggers `disburse_stipend_saga`\n3. **Projections Service** subscribes to all events, folds into SQLite dashboard\n\n### Command-Query Separation\n\n**Write Path:**\n- HTTP POST /tuition/pay → Command Bus → Use Case → Domain Logic → Event Store\n\n**Read Path:**\n- HTTP GET /dashboard → Query Bus → Read Model (SQLite) → JSON Presenter\n\n**Benefits:**\n- Different persistence for writes (event log) vs. reads (denormalized index)\n- Projections lag writes but scale reads independently\n- Eventually consistent dashboards acceptable for reporting\n\n---\n\n## 11. DEPLOYMENT & EXTENSIBILITY\n\n### Swappable Infrastructure Examples\n\n**1. Replace Event Store:**\n```c\n// Today: PostgreSQL\nstruct event_store *store = postgres_event_store_create(...);\n\n// Tomorrow: SQLite (for lab exercises)\nstruct event_store *store = sqlite_event_store_create(...);\n\n// Code using store unchanged:\nevent_store_append(store, stream, events, count, version);\n```\n\n**2. Replace Message Broker:**\n```c\n// Today: STDIN/STDOUT (pedagogical)\nvoid broker_publish(const Message *msg) { fprintf(stdout, ...); }\n\n// Tomorrow: RabbitMQ\nvoid broker_publish(const Message *msg) { amqp_basic_publish(...); }\n\n// Bursar service unchanged:\nif (!process_message(&msg)) break;\n```\n\n**3. Replace Crypto:**\n- Default: AES-256-GCM (OpenSSL)\n- Lab: XOR cipher (insecure, for teaching only)\n- Production: Hardware security module (HSM) via PKCS#11\n\n---\n\n## 12. TESTING STRATEGY\n\n### Unit Tests (Domain Logic)\n- No I/O dependencies\n- Pure C functions\n- Mock ports injected\n\n### Integration Tests\n- Services in-process\n- Fake message broker (memory buffer)\n- SQLite instead of PostgreSQL\n\n### End-to-End Tests\n- Docker Compose (full stack)\n- Real PostgreSQL + RabbitMQ\n- Chaos injection (kill services mid-saga)\n\n---\n\n## FILE PATH All side-effects are routed through\n *  a set of callbacks supplied by the calling application/service.  Each\n *  callback must be idempotent because the saga might retry crashed steps.\n *\n *  Every callback returns `true` on success.  On failure it returns `false`\n *  and must populate `err_out`.\n */\n\ntypedef bool (*validate_eligibility_fn)(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n        saga_error_t                         *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*reserve_budget_fn)(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n        uint64_t                             *reservation_id_out,\n        saga_error_t                         *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*release_budget_fn)(\n        uint64_t       reservation_id,\n        saga_error_t  *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*initiate_transfer_fn)(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n        uint64_t                             *network_tx_id_out,\n        saga_error_t                         *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*reverse_transfer_fn)(\n        uint64_t       network_tx_id,\n        saga_error_t  *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*record_ledger_entry_fn)(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n        const char                           *entry_type,\n        uint64_t                              related_id, /* reservation or tx id */\n        saga_error_t                         *err_out);\n\ntypedef bool (*publish_domain_event_fn)(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n        const char                           *event_name,\n        saga_error_t                         *err_out);\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*  VTable Container                                                          */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\ntypedef struct {\n    validate_eligibility_fn   validate_eligibility;\n    reserve_budget_fn         reserve_budget;\n    release_budget_fn         release_budget;\n    initiate_transfer_fn      initiate_transfer;\n    reverse_transfer_fn       reverse_transfer;\n    record_ledger_entry_fn    record_ledger_entry;\n    publish_domain_event_fn   publish_domain_event;\n} stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t;\n\n/* Utility macro to check if a pointer in the vtable is missing at runtime. */\n#define _CHECK_CB(cb) do {                                                  \\\n    if ((cb) == NULL) {                                                     \\\n        if (err_out) {                                                      \\\n            err_out->code = SAGA_ERR_INTERNAL;                              \\\n            snprintf(err_out->message, sizeof(err_out->message),            \\\n                     \"Callback missing: %s\", #cb);                          \\\n        }                                                                   \\\n        return false;                                                       \\\n    } } while (0)\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*  Saga Public API                                                           */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\nstatic inline\nbool disburse_stipend_run(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t   *req,\n        const stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t *cb,\n        saga_error_t                           *err_out);\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*  Implementation                                                            */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\nstatic inline\nbool disburse_stipend_run(\n        const stipend_disbursement_request_t   *req,\n        const stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t *cb,\n        saga_error_t                           *err_out)\n{\n    /* Defensive programming: never trust caller. */\n    if (!req || !cb) {\n        if (err_out) {\n            err_out->code = SAGA_ERR_INTERNAL;\n            snprintf(err_out->message, sizeof(err_out->message),\n                     \"NULL argument(s) received\");\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    uint64_t reservation_id = 0;\n    uint64_t network_tx_id  = 0;\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 1. Validate eligibility                                            */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    _CHECK_CB(cb->validate_eligibility);\n    if (!cb->validate_eligibility(req, err_out)) {\n        return false; /* validation errors are final, no compensation needed */\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 2. Reserve budget (Financial-Aid allocation ledger)                */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    _CHECK_CB(cb->reserve_budget);\n    if (!cb->reserve_budget(req, &reservation_id, err_out)) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 3. Initiate transfer via payment network                           */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    _CHECK_CB(cb->initiate_transfer);\n    if (!cb->initiate_transfer(req, &network_tx_id, err_out)) {\n\n        /* ------------------------------------------------------------------\n         * Compensation Path #1: release the budget reservation\n         * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->release_budget);\n        saga_error_t cmp_err = SAGA_ERROR_INIT();\n        if (!cb->release_budget(reservation_id, &cmp_err)) {\n            /* Fatal: both forward step + compensation failed */\n            if (err_out && err_out->code == SAGA_OK) {\n                *err_out = cmp_err;\n            }\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        return false; /* original error already populated by initiate_transfer */\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 4. Record ledger entry (financial book-keeping)                    */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    _CHECK_CB(cb->record_ledger_entry);\n    if (!cb->record_ledger_entry(req, \"STIPEND_DISBURSEMENT\", network_tx_id, err_out)) {\n\n        /* Compensation Path #2: reverse transfer + release budget */\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->reverse_transfer);\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->release_budget);\n\n        saga_error_t cmp_err1 = SAGA_ERROR_INIT();\n        if (!cb->reverse_transfer(network_tx_id, &cmp_err1)) {\n            if (err_out && err_out->code == SAGA_OK) *err_out = cmp_err1;\n            /* Deliberately fall through to attempt second compensation */\n        }\n\n        saga_error_t cmp_err2 = SAGA_ERROR_INIT();\n        if (!cb->release_budget(reservation_id, &cmp_err2)) {\n            if (err_out && err_out->code == SAGA_OK) *err_out = cmp_err2;\n        }\n\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 5. Publish domain event (CQRS / Integration-Event)                 */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    _CHECK_CB(cb->publish_domain_event);\n    if (!cb->publish_domain_event(req, \"StipendDisbursed\", err_out)) {\n\n        /* Compensation Path #3: ledger reversal + reverse transfer + release budget */\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->record_ledger_entry);\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->reverse_transfer);\n        _CHECK_CB(cb->release_budget);\n\n        saga_error_t cmp_err = SAGA_ERROR_INIT();\n        cb->record_ledger_entry(req, \"STIPEND_DISBURSEMENT_REVERSED\", network_tx_id, &cmp_err);\n        cb->reverse_transfer(network_tx_id, &cmp_err);\n        cb->release_budget(reservation_id, &cmp_err);\n\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    /* 6. Success!                                                        */\n    /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n    if (err_out) {\n        *err_out = SAGA_ERROR_INIT(); /* reset to success */\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*  Example: Default No-Op Callbacks for Unit-Testing                         */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n#ifdef EDUPAY_STIPEND_SAGA_ENABLE_NOOP_CALLBACKS\n\n#include <string.h>\n\n/* These compile-time helpers allow professors to run the saga in a REPL\n * without wiring external systems.  Each callback prints to stdout and\n * pretends to succeed.\n */\n\nstatic inline bool noop_validate(const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)req; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] validate_eligibility\");\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_reserve(const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n                                uint64_t *id, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)req; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] reserve_budget\");\n    *id = 42;\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_release(uint64_t id, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)id; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] release_budget\");\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_transfer(const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n                                 uint64_t *id, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)req; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] initiate_transfer\");\n    *id = 1337;\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_reverse(uint64_t id, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)id; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] reverse_transfer\");\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_ledger(const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n                               const char *type, uint64_t rel_id, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)req; (void)type; (void)rel_id; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] record_ledger_entry\");\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic inline bool noop_publish(const stipend_disbursement_request_t *req,\n                                const char *name, saga_error_t *err) {\n    (void)req; (void)name; (void)err;\n    puts(\"[noop] publish_domain_event\");\n    return true;\n}\n\nstatic const stipend_disbursement_callbacks_t STIPEND_SAGA_NOOP_CALLBACKS = {\n    .validate_eligibility = noop_validate,\n    .reserve_budget       = noop_reserve,\n    .release_budget       = noop_release,\n    .initiate_transfer    = noop_transfer,\n    .reverse_transfer     = noop_reverse,\n    .record_ledger_entry  = noop_ledger,\n    .publish_domain_event = noop_publish\n};\n\n#endif /* EDUPAY_STIPEND_SAGA_ENABLE_NOOP_CALLBACKS */\n\n#endif /* EDUPAY_LEDGER_ACADEMY_FINANCIAL_AID_DISBURSE_STIPEND_SAGA_H */\n","numLines":362,"startLine":1,"totalLines":362}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EGEa21bpvUbqkTyjfYnoW4","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# EduPay Ledger Academy  \n     3→### docs/architecture/04_saga_pattern.md\n     4→---\n     5→> Version: 1.3  \n     6→> Last Reviewed: 2024-05-09  \n     7→> Authors: Core Platform Team (@edupay-ledger)  \n     8→> Status: ✅ Production  \n     9→---\n    10→\n    11→## 1. Why EduPay Uses the Saga Pattern\n    12→\n    13→EduPay processes **millions of distributed, financial micro-transactions** each semester.  \n    14→The platform deliberately avoids *single-phase* database transactions because:\n    15→\n    16→* **Micro-services** compose payments from multiple bounded contexts (Admissions, Bursar, Treasury, FX, Fraud).  \n    17→* **Heterogeneous data stores** (PostgreSQL, Redis, EventStoreDB) participate in a single business flow.  \n    18→* **Resiliency labs** in coursework require controlled failure injection and roll-back.  \n    19→\n    20→A **Saga** slices a long-running business process into a series of *local* ACID transactions plus **compensating steps**.  \n    21→If any step fails, the orchestrator triggers rollback actions in **reverse order**, yielding *eventual consistency* without locking global resources.\n    22→\n    23→```\n    24→Enrollment_Fee → FX_Conversion → Treasury_Posting → Fraud_Scoring → Receipt_Email\n    25→         │                │                │              │\n    26→         └────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────┴─────┐\n    27→                       Saga Orchestrator (payment_saga.c)       │\n    28→                       • persists state in Audit_Trail          │\n    29→                       • broadcasts events over EventBus        │\n    30→                       • drives rollbacks on failure ◄──────────┘\n    31→```\n    32→\n    33→---\n    34→\n    35→## 2. High-Level Flow\n    36→\n    37→1. **Create** a `payment_saga_t` record in the `audit_trail` stream.  \n    38→2. **Send** `SAGA_STEP_BEGIN` for *FX_Conversion* (`fx.c`).  \n    39→3. **Local commit** in FX; publish `SAGA_STEP_DONE`.  \n    40→4. Orchestrator continues to *Treasury_Posting* (`ledger.c`).  \n    41→5. If *Fraud_Scoring* fails, **publish** `SAGA_ABORT`.  \n    42→6. Participants receiving `SAGA_ABORT` invoke *compensating* handlers.  \n    43→7. Saga ends with `SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE` or `SAGA_SUCCESS`.\n    44→\n    45→---\n    46→\n    47→## 3. Reference Implementation (C)\n    48→\n    49→All code is kept framework-agnostic, consistent with **Clean Architecture**.  \n    50→Dependencies limited to the standard C library (`pthread` for concurrency).\n    51→\n    52→### 3.1. Public Interface – `include/saga.h`\n    53→\n    54→```c\n    55→#ifndef EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n    56→#define EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n    57→\n    58→#include <stdint.h>\n    59→#include <stdbool.h>\n    60→\n    61→/* ---------- Event Bus ---------------------------------------------------- */\n    62→\n    63→typedef enum {\n    64→    EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN,\n    65→    EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE,\n    66→    EVT_SAGA_ABORT,\n    67→    EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n    68→    EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS\n    69→} saga_event_type_t;\n    70→\n    71→typedef struct {\n    72→    saga_event_type_t type;\n    73→    uint64_t          saga_id;\n    74→    const char       *step_name;   /* e.g., \"FX_Conversion\" */\n    75→    void             *payload;     /* opaque domain state   */\n    76→} saga_event_t;\n    77→\n    78→/* Subscriber callback signature */\n    79→typedef void (*event_handler_f)(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx);\n    80→\n    81→/* Registers a handler; returns 0 on success, -1 on error */\n    82→int eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx);\n    83→\n    84→/* Broadcasts event to all subscribers; thread-safe */\n    85→int eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt);\n    86→\n    87→/* ---------- Saga Orchestrator ------------------------------------------- */\n    88→\n    89→#define SAGA_MAX_STEPS 8\n    90→#define SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX 32\n    91→\n    92→typedef int (*saga_step_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void **payload);\n    93→typedef int (*saga_comp_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void *payload);\n    94→\n    95→typedef struct {\n    96→    char         name[SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX];\n    97→    saga_step_f  action;\n    98→    saga_comp_f  compensate;\n    99→} saga_step_def_t;\n   100→\n   101→typedef struct {\n   102→    uint64_t        id;\n   103→    uint8_t         step_count;\n   104→    uint8_t         current;\n   105→    saga_step_def_t steps[SAGA_MAX_STEPS];\n   106→    bool            aborted;\n   107→} payment_saga_t;\n   108→\n   109→/* Initializes a saga with the supplied steps */\n   110→void saga_init(payment_saga_t *saga, uint64_t id,\n   111→               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n   112→               uint8_t step_count);\n   113→\n   114→/* Executes saga synchronously; returns 0 on success, -1 on failure */\n   115→int saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga);\n   116→\n   117→#endif /* EDUPAY_SAGA_H */\n   118→```\n   119→\n   120→### 3.2. Core Implementation – `src/saga.c`\n   121→\n   122→```c\n   123→#include \"saga.h\"\n   124→#include <pthread.h>\n   125→#include <stdio.h>\n   126→#include <stdlib.h>\n   127→#include <string.h>\n   128→\n   129→#define BUS_MAX_HANDLERS 16\n   130→\n   131→/* ----------------- Simple In-Process Event Bus -------------------------- */\n   132→\n   133→typedef struct {\n   134→    event_handler_f cb;\n   135→    void           *ctx;\n   136→} handler_entry_t;\n   137→\n   138→static handler_entry_t BUS[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\n   139→static pthread_mutex_t BUS_LOCK = PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER;\n   140→\n   141→int eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx)\n   142→{\n   143→    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   144→    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n   145→        if (BUS[i].cb == NULL) {\n   146→            BUS[i].cb  = cb;\n   147→            BUS[i].ctx = ctx;\n   148→            pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   149→            return 0;\n   150→        }\n   151→    }\n   152→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   153→    return -1; /* No slots */\n   154→}\n   155→\n   156→int eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt)\n   157→{\n   158→    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   159→    handler_entry_t handlers[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\n   160→    memcpy(handlers, BUS, sizeof(handlers));\n   161→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n   162→\n   163→    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n   164→        if (handlers[i].cb)\n   165→            handlers[i].cb(evt, handlers[i].ctx);\n   166→    }\n   167→    return 0;\n   168→}\n   169→\n   170→/* ----------------- Saga Orchestration ---------------------------------- */\n   171→\n   172→static void publish_step_event(saga_event_type_t type,\n   173→                               const payment_saga_t *saga,\n   174→                               const char *step_name,\n   175→                               void *payload)\n   176→{\n   177→    saga_event_t evt = {\n   178→        .type      = type,\n   179→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   180→        .step_name = step_name,\n   181→        .payload   = payload\n   182→    };\n   183→    eventbus_publish(&evt);\n   184→}\n   185→\n   186→void saga_init(payment_saga_t *s, uint64_t id,\n   187→               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n   188→               uint8_t step_count)\n   189→{\n   190→    memset(s, 0, sizeof(*s));\n   191→    s->id         = id;\n   192→    s->step_count = step_count > SAGA_MAX_STEPS ? SAGA_MAX_STEPS : step_count;\n   193→    memcpy(s->steps, steps, s->step_count * sizeof(saga_step_def_t));\n   194→}\n   195→\n   196→static int rollback(payment_saga_t *saga)\n   197→{\n   198→    for (int i = saga->current; i >= 0; --i) {\n   199→        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[i];\n   200→        if (step->compensate) {\n   201→            publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_ABORT, saga, step->name, NULL);\n   202→            if (step->compensate(saga->id, NULL) != 0)\n   203→                fprintf(stderr, \"⚠  Compensation failed at %s\\n\", step->name);\n   204→        }\n   205→    }\n   206→    saga_event_t done = {\n   207→        .type      = EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n   208→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   209→        .step_name = \"ROLLBACK\"\n   210→    };\n   211→    eventbus_publish(&done);\n   212→    return -1;\n   213→}\n   214→\n   215→int saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga)\n   216→{\n   217→    for (saga->current = 0; saga->current < saga->step_count; ++saga->current) {\n   218→        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[saga->current];\n   219→\n   220→        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN, saga, step->name, NULL);\n   221→\n   222→        void *payload = NULL;\n   223→        if (step->action(saga->id, &payload) != 0) {\n   224→            fprintf(stderr, \"❌  Saga %lu failed at step %s\\n\",\n   225→                    saga->id, step->name);\n   226→            saga->aborted = true;\n   227→            return rollback(saga);\n   228→        }\n   229→\n   230→        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE, saga, step->name, payload);\n   231→    }\n   232→\n   233→    saga_event_t ok = {\n   234→        .type      = EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS,\n   235→        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n   236→        .step_name = \"COMPLETED\"\n   237→    };\n   238→    eventbus_publish(&ok);\n   239→    return 0;\n   240→}\n   241→```\n   242→\n   243→### 3.3. Example Steps – `src/steps_fx.c`, `src/steps_ledger.c`\n   244→\n   245→```c\n   246→/* src/steps_fx.c */\n   247→#include \"saga.h\"\n   248→#include <stdio.h>\n   249→\n   250→int fx_convert(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   251→{\n   252→    (void)payload; /* Not needed for demo */\n   253→    printf(\"💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   254→    /* simulate success */\n   255→    return 0;\n   256→}\n   257→\n   258→int fx_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   259→{\n   260→    (void)payload;\n   261→    printf(\"↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   262→    return 0;\n   263→}\n   264→```\n   265→\n   266→```c\n   267→/* src/steps_ledger.c */\n   268→#include \"saga.h\"\n   269→#include <stdio.h>\n   270→\n   271→int ledger_post(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   272→{\n   273→    (void)payload;\n   274→    printf(\"📒  Ledger posting OK for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   275→    return 0;\n   276→}\n   277→\n   278→int ledger_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   279→{\n   280→    (void)payload;\n   281→    printf(\"↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   282→    return 0;\n   283→}\n   284→```\n   285→\n   286→### 3.4. Demonstration Driver – `samples/payment_saga.c`\n   287→\n   288→```c\n   289→#include \"saga.h\"\n   290→#include <stdio.h>\n   291→#include <stdlib.h>\n   292→#include <time.h>\n   293→\n   294→/* ---------- Step Implementations --------------------------------------- */\n   295→extern int fx_convert(uint64_t, void **);\n   296→extern int fx_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n   297→\n   298→extern int ledger_post(uint64_t, void **);\n   299→extern int ledger_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n   300→\n   301→static int fraud_score(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n   302→{\n   303→    (void)payload;\n   304→    printf(\"🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   305→\n   306→    /* Simulate 30% failure rate for classroom chaos monkeys */\n   307→    if (rand() % 10 < 3) {\n   308→        fprintf(stderr, \"🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\\n\");\n   309→        return -1;\n   310→    }\n   311→\n   312→    printf(\"✅  Fraud scoring passed\\n\");\n   313→    return 0;\n   314→}\n   315→\n   316→static int fraud_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n   317→{\n   318→    (void)payload;\n   319→    printf(\"↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n   320→    return 0;\n   321→}\n   322→\n   323→/* ---------- Audit Trail Console Subscriber ----------------------------- */\n   324→static void audit_logger(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx)\n   325→{\n   326→    (void)ctx;\n   327→    const char *type_str[] = {\n   328→        \"STEP_BEGIN\", \"STEP_DONE\", \"ABORT\", \"ROLLBACK_DONE\", \"SUCCESS\"\n   329→    };\n   330→    printf(\"📝  Audit: %s — saga=%lu step=%s\\n\",\n   331→           type_str[evt->type], evt->saga_id, evt->step_name);\n   332→}\n   333→\n   334→int main(void)\n   335→{\n   336→    srand((unsigned)time(NULL));\n   337→    eventbus_subscribe(audit_logger, NULL);\n   338→\n   339→    saga_step_def_t steps[] = {\n   340→        { \"FX_Conversion\",   fx_convert,   fx_reverse   },\n   341→        { \"Ledger_Posting\",  ledger_post,  ledger_reverse },\n   342→        { \"Fraud_Scoring\",   fraud_score,  fraud_reverse }\n   343→    };\n   344→\n   345→    payment_saga_t saga;\n   346→    saga_init(&saga, 42, steps, 3);\n   347→\n   348→    int rc = saga_execute(&saga);\n   349→    printf(rc == 0 ? \"🎉  Saga completed successfully!\\n\"\n   350→                   : \"💔  Saga rolled back.\\n\");\n   351→    return rc;\n   352→}\n   353→```\n   354→\n   355→Compile & Run:\n   356→\n   357→```bash\n   358→gcc -Iinclude src/saga.c src/steps_fx.c src/steps_ledger.c samples/payment_saga.c -lpthread -o bin/payment_saga\n   359→./bin/payment_saga\n   360→```\n   361→\n   362→Expected terminal output (with occasional induced failure):\n   363→\n   364→```\n   365→💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga 42\n   366→📒  Ledger posting OK for saga 42\n   367→🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga 42\n   368→🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\n   369→❌  Saga 42 failed at step Fraud_Scoring\n   370→↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga 42\n   371→↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga 42\n   372→↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga 42\n   373→💔  Saga rolled back.\n   374→```\n   375→\n   376→---\n   377→\n   378→## 4. Teaching Notes\n   379→\n   380→* The orchestrator resides **inside** the Payment bounded context, not in an external workflow engine.  \n   381→  Code can therefore be debugged with `gdb` by novices.\n   382→* Because the event bus is *in-process*, instructors can swap it for a\n   383→  real Kafka / NATS adapter during advanced labs without touching business logic.\n   384→* Compensations are **idempotent**: every reverse handler can run twice safely,\n   385→  a mandatory property in at-least-once delivery environments.\n   386→\n   387→---\n   388→\n   389→## 5. Production Hardening Checklist\n   390→\n   391→- [x] Persist `payment_saga_t` progress to EventStoreDB → replay after crash.  \n   392→- [x] Encrypt payloads (`payload_encrypt()`) for PCI-DSS compliance.  \n   393→- [x] Emit OpenTelemetry spans for distributed tracing.  \n   394→- [ ] Add circuit-breakers around downstream services (coming Q3).  \n   395→\n   396→---\n   397→\n   398→Happy hacking!  \n   399→EduPay Ledger Team ✨\n   400→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Why EduPay Uses the Saga Pattern\n\nEduPay processes **millions of distributed, financial micro-transactions** each semester.  \nThe platform deliberately avoids *single-phase* database transactions because:\n\n* **Micro-services** compose payments from multiple bounded contexts (Admissions, Bursar, Treasury, FX, Fraud).  \n* **Heterogeneous data stores** (PostgreSQL, Redis, EventStoreDB) participate in a single business flow.  \n* **Resiliency labs** in coursework require controlled failure injection and roll-back.  \n\nA **Saga** slices a long-running business process into a series of *local* ACID transactions plus **compensating steps**.  \nIf any step fails, the orchestrator triggers rollback actions in **reverse order**, yielding *eventual consistency* without locking global resources.\n\n```\nEnrollment_Fee → FX_Conversion → Treasury_Posting → Fraud_Scoring → Receipt_Email\n         │                │                │              │\n         └────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────┴─────┐\n                       Saga Orchestrator (payment_saga.c)       │\n                       • persists state in Audit_Trail          │\n                       • broadcasts events over EventBus        │\n                       • drives rollbacks on failure ◄──────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. High-Level Flow\n\n1. **Create** a `payment_saga_t` record in the `audit_trail` stream.  \n2. **Send** `SAGA_STEP_BEGIN` for *FX_Conversion* (`fx.c`).  \n3. **Local commit** in FX; publish `SAGA_STEP_DONE`.  \n4. Orchestrator continues to *Treasury_Posting* (`ledger.c`).  \n5. If *Fraud_Scoring* fails, **publish** `SAGA_ABORT`.  \n6. Participants receiving `SAGA_ABORT` invoke *compensating* handlers.  \n7. Saga ends with `SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE` or `SAGA_SUCCESS`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Reference Implementation (C)\n\nAll code is kept framework-agnostic, consistent with **Clean Architecture**.  \nDependencies limited to the standard C library (`pthread` for concurrency).\n\n### 3.1. Public Interface – `include/saga.h`\n\n```c\n#ifndef EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n#define EDUPAY_SAGA_H\n\n#include <stdint.h>\n#include <stdbool.h>\n\n/* ---------- Event Bus ---------------------------------------------------- */\n\ntypedef enum {\n    EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN,\n    EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE,\n    EVT_SAGA_ABORT,\n    EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n    EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS\n} saga_event_type_t;\n\ntypedef struct {\n    saga_event_type_t type;\n    uint64_t          saga_id;\n    const char       *step_name;   /* e.g., \"FX_Conversion\" */\n    void             *payload;     /* opaque domain state   */\n} saga_event_t;\n\n/* Subscriber callback signature */\ntypedef void (*event_handler_f)(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx);\n\n/* Registers a handler; returns 0 on success, -1 on error */\nint eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx);\n\n/* Broadcasts event to all subscribers; thread-safe */\nint eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt);\n\n/* ---------- Saga Orchestrator ------------------------------------------- */\n\n#define SAGA_MAX_STEPS 8\n#define SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX 32\n\ntypedef int (*saga_step_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void **payload);\ntypedef int (*saga_comp_f)(uint64_t saga_id, void *payload);\n\ntypedef struct {\n    char         name[SAGA_STEP_NAME_MAX];\n    saga_step_f  action;\n    saga_comp_f  compensate;\n} saga_step_def_t;\n\ntypedef struct {\n    uint64_t        id;\n    uint8_t         step_count;\n    uint8_t         current;\n    saga_step_def_t steps[SAGA_MAX_STEPS];\n    bool            aborted;\n} payment_saga_t;\n\n/* Initializes a saga with the supplied steps */\nvoid saga_init(payment_saga_t *saga, uint64_t id,\n               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n               uint8_t step_count);\n\n/* Executes saga synchronously; returns 0 on success, -1 on failure */\nint saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga);\n\n#endif /* EDUPAY_SAGA_H */\n```\n\n### 3.2. Core Implementation – `src/saga.c`\n\n```c\n#include \"saga.h\"\n#include <pthread.h>\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <string.h>\n\n#define BUS_MAX_HANDLERS 16\n\n/* ----------------- Simple In-Process Event Bus -------------------------- */\n\ntypedef struct {\n    event_handler_f cb;\n    void           *ctx;\n} handler_entry_t;\n\nstatic handler_entry_t BUS[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\nstatic pthread_mutex_t BUS_LOCK = PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER;\n\nint eventbus_subscribe(event_handler_f cb, void *ctx)\n{\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n        if (BUS[i].cb == NULL) {\n            BUS[i].cb  = cb;\n            BUS[i].ctx = ctx;\n            pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n            return 0;\n        }\n    }\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n    return -1; /* No slots */\n}\n\nint eventbus_publish(const saga_event_t *evt)\n{\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&BUS_LOCK);\n    handler_entry_t handlers[BUS_MAX_HANDLERS];\n    memcpy(handlers, BUS, sizeof(handlers));\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&BUS_LOCK);\n\n    for (int i = 0; i < BUS_MAX_HANDLERS; ++i) {\n        if (handlers[i].cb)\n            handlers[i].cb(evt, handlers[i].ctx);\n    }\n    return 0;\n}\n\n/* ----------------- Saga Orchestration ---------------------------------- */\n\nstatic void publish_step_event(saga_event_type_t type,\n                               const payment_saga_t *saga,\n                               const char *step_name,\n                               void *payload)\n{\n    saga_event_t evt = {\n        .type      = type,\n        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n        .step_name = step_name,\n        .payload   = payload\n    };\n    eventbus_publish(&evt);\n}\n\nvoid saga_init(payment_saga_t *s, uint64_t id,\n               const saga_step_def_t *steps,\n               uint8_t step_count)\n{\n    memset(s, 0, sizeof(*s));\n    s->id         = id;\n    s->step_count = step_count > SAGA_MAX_STEPS ? SAGA_MAX_STEPS : step_count;\n    memcpy(s->steps, steps, s->step_count * sizeof(saga_step_def_t));\n}\n\nstatic int rollback(payment_saga_t *saga)\n{\n    for (int i = saga->current; i >= 0; --i) {\n        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[i];\n        if (step->compensate) {\n            publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_ABORT, saga, step->name, NULL);\n            if (step->compensate(saga->id, NULL) != 0)\n                fprintf(stderr, \"⚠  Compensation failed at %s\\n\", step->name);\n        }\n    }\n    saga_event_t done = {\n        .type      = EVT_SAGA_ROLLBACK_DONE,\n        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n        .step_name = \"ROLLBACK\"\n    };\n    eventbus_publish(&done);\n    return -1;\n}\n\nint saga_execute(payment_saga_t *saga)\n{\n    for (saga->current = 0; saga->current < saga->step_count; ++saga->current) {\n        saga_step_def_t *step = &saga->steps[saga->current];\n\n        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_BEGIN, saga, step->name, NULL);\n\n        void *payload = NULL;\n        if (step->action(saga->id, &payload) != 0) {\n            fprintf(stderr, \"❌  Saga %lu failed at step %s\\n\",\n                    saga->id, step->name);\n            saga->aborted = true;\n            return rollback(saga);\n        }\n\n        publish_step_event(EVT_SAGA_STEP_DONE, saga, step->name, payload);\n    }\n\n    saga_event_t ok = {\n        .type      = EVT_SAGA_SUCCESS,\n        .saga_id   = saga->id,\n        .step_name = \"COMPLETED\"\n    };\n    eventbus_publish(&ok);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n### 3.3. Example Steps – `src/steps_fx.c`, `src/steps_ledger.c`\n\n```c\n/* src/steps_fx.c */\n#include \"saga.h\"\n#include <stdio.h>\n\nint fx_convert(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n{\n    (void)payload; /* Not needed for demo */\n    printf(\"💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n    /* simulate success */\n    return 0;\n}\n\nint fx_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n{\n    (void)payload;\n    printf(\"↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n```c\n/* src/steps_ledger.c */\n#include \"saga.h\"\n#include <stdio.h>\n\nint ledger_post(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n{\n    (void)payload;\n    printf(\"📒  Ledger posting OK for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nint ledger_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n{\n    (void)payload;\n    printf(\"↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n### 3.4. Demonstration Driver – `samples/payment_saga.c`\n\n```c\n#include \"saga.h\"\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <time.h>\n\n/* ---------- Step Implementations --------------------------------------- */\nextern int fx_convert(uint64_t, void **);\nextern int fx_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n\nextern int ledger_post(uint64_t, void **);\nextern int ledger_reverse(uint64_t, void *);\n\nstatic int fraud_score(uint64_t id, void **payload)\n{\n    (void)payload;\n    printf(\"🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n\n    /* Simulate 30% failure rate for classroom chaos monkeys */\n    if (rand() % 10 < 3) {\n        fprintf(stderr, \"🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\\n\");\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    printf(\"✅  Fraud scoring passed\\n\");\n    return 0;\n}\n\nstatic int fraud_reverse(uint64_t id, void *payload)\n{\n    (void)payload;\n    printf(\"↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga %lu\\n\", id);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n/* ---------- Audit Trail Console Subscriber ----------------------------- */\nstatic void audit_logger(const saga_event_t *evt, void *ctx)\n{\n    (void)ctx;\n    const char *type_str[] = {\n        \"STEP_BEGIN\", \"STEP_DONE\", \"ABORT\", \"ROLLBACK_DONE\", \"SUCCESS\"\n    };\n    printf(\"📝  Audit: %s — saga=%lu step=%s\\n\",\n           type_str[evt->type], evt->saga_id, evt->step_name);\n}\n\nint main(void)\n{\n    srand((unsigned)time(NULL));\n    eventbus_subscribe(audit_logger, NULL);\n\n    saga_step_def_t steps[] = {\n        { \"FX_Conversion\",   fx_convert,   fx_reverse   },\n        { \"Ledger_Posting\",  ledger_post,  ledger_reverse },\n        { \"Fraud_Scoring\",   fraud_score,  fraud_reverse }\n    };\n\n    payment_saga_t saga;\n    saga_init(&saga, 42, steps, 3);\n\n    int rc = saga_execute(&saga);\n    printf(rc == 0 ? \"🎉  Saga completed successfully!\\n\"\n                   : \"💔  Saga rolled back.\\n\");\n    return rc;\n}\n```\n\nCompile & Run:\n\n```bash\ngcc -Iinclude src/saga.c src/steps_fx.c src/steps_ledger.c samples/payment_saga.c -lpthread -o bin/payment_saga\n./bin/payment_saga\n```\n\nExpected terminal output (with occasional induced failure):\n\n```\n💱  FX_Conversion performed for saga 42\n📒  Ledger posting OK for saga 42\n🕵  Fraud scoring started for saga 42\n🚨  Fraud scoring FAILED (suspicious)\n❌  Saga 42 failed at step Fraud_Scoring\n↩  Fraud scoring compensation (unflag) for saga 42\n↩  Ledger posting reversed for saga 42\n↩  FX_Conversion reversed for saga 42\n💔  Saga rolled back.\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Teaching Notes\n\n* The orchestrator resides **inside** the Payment bounded context, not in an external workflow engine.  \n  Code can therefore be debugged with `gdb` by novices.\n* Because the event bus is *in-process*, instructors can swap it for a\n  real Kafka / NATS adapter during advanced labs without touching business logic.\n* Compensations are **idempotent**: every reverse handler can run twice safely,\n  a mandatory property in at-least-once delivery environments.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Production Hardening Checklist\n\n- [x] Persist `payment_saga_t` progress to EventStoreDB → replay after crash.  \n- [x] Encrypt payloads (`payload_encrypt()`) for PCI-DSS compliance.  \n- [x] Emit OpenTelemetry spans for distributed tracing.  \n- [ ] Add circuit-breakers around downstream services (coming Q3).  \n\n---\n\nHappy hacking!  \nEduPay Ledger Team ✨\n```","numLines":400,"startLine":1,"totalLines":400}}}
